L401: ## **Creatine Phosphokinase / Creatine Kinase**
L402: Five participants (22%) in the nedosiran group and two (17%) in the placebo group had an increase in CPK above the ULN, as shown in Table 31. Four of these participants in the nedosiran group (17%) and none in placebo had a CPK &gt;3x ULN. None of the participants had symptoms of muscle pain or weakness, all remained on nedosiran treatment, and CPK in all but one participant (b) (6) returned to normal without additional intervention, suggesting that nedosiran did not play a role. Participant (b) (6) had fluctuating CK levels that could have been related to exercise (see the narrative below).
L403: - **Participant** (b) (6): An 18-year-old male with history of PH1, kidney stones, lithotripsy, gout, and stage 2 CKD. The participant was on concomitant allopurinol, colchicine, ibuprofen, potassium citrate, and pyridoxine. The participant was randomized to nedosiran. On (b) (6) the participant's Baseline CK was 100 U/L (ULN 200 U/L). On Day 45 (b) (6), after receiving two doses of nedosiran, CK increased to 1416 U/L (7x ULN). AEs reported around the same time included non-cardiac chest pain and exacerbation of gout on Day 46 (b) (6). The gout was treated with colchicine. On Day 60 (b) (6) repeat CK was 68 U/L and remained within the normal range thereafter until the end of the study. Nedosiran dosing was not changed or interrupted because of the elevated CK. The participant received five of six scheduled nedosiran doses in DCR-PHXC-201 from (b) (6) to (b) (6) (Day 90 dose missed because of coronavirus disease 2019 (COVID-19)-related travel restrictions). He rolled over to DCR-PHXC-301 and continued nedosiran from (b) (6) (Day 1) to Day 60 (3 doses). CPK remained normal during participation in DCR-PHXC-301. The participant discontinued participation in the study because he moved to another country and could not return to the United States because of COVID-19 travel restrictions.

- **Participant** (b) (6): A 41-year-old male with history of PH1, urinary stones, lithotripsy, kidney stone removal, urethral repair, dry eye, hypertension, and Wolff-Parkinson-White syndrome on concomitant hyaluronate sodium eye drops, paracetamol, ramipril, and pyridoxine. On (b) (6) the participant's baseline CK was elevated at 355 U/L (ULN 200 U/L). On Day 15 (b) (6) 14 days after the first dose of nedosiran, CK transiently increased to 837 U/L (4x ULN) but decreased to 394 U/L on Day 30. On Day 120 (b) (6) CK transiently increased to 3099 U/L (15x ULN); it decreased to 605 U/L when rechecked on (b) (6) and had returned to around baseline at 377 U/L on Day 150 (b) (6) without intervention. The participant's end-of-study (Day 180, (b) (6) CK was 499 U/L. Nedosiran dosing was not changed or interrupted during the study.

The patient subsequently rolled over to DCR-PHXC-301; all available CK levels to data cutoff have been elevated at 419 U/L on Day 30, 310 U/L on Day 90, and 1225 U/L (6x ULN) on Day 180. The participant reported rock climbing and that he had started exercising.
L404: - **Participant** (b) (6): A 16-year-old male with a history of PH1, hypomagnesemia, vitamin D deficiency, post-traumatic stress disorder, tinea capitis, and obesity. The participant was on concomitant cholecalciferol, magnesium citrate, pyridoxine, topical ketoconazole, and topical betamethasone dipropionate-salicylic acid. The participant received six scheduled nedosiran doses in DCR-PHXC-201 from (b) (6) to (b) (6). The participant had an increase in CPK to &gt;3x ULN while in DCR-PHXC-201.

Baseline CPK in DCR-PHXC-201 was mildly elevated at 268 U/L (normal range 2 to 251 U/L). One day after the first nedosiran dose, CPK was 142 U/L. On Day 30, CPK increased to 940 U/L (3.7x ULN) and was associated with an AE of fatigue, then down trended to normal by Day 45 and remained within the normal range thereafter, including from Baseline to Month 10 in DCR-PHXC-301. Nedosiran dosing was not interrupted.
- **Participant** (b) (6): A 36-year-old male with history of PH1, hypertension, kidney stones, kidney stone removal, and ureteral stent placement. The participant was on concomitant magnesium, potassium citrate, ramipril, and pyridoxine. The participant had an increase in CPK to &gt;3x ULN in DCR-PHXC-201 and to &gt;10x ULN in DCR-PHXC-301.

The participant received six doses of nedosiran from (b) (6) to (b) (6) in DCR-PHXC-201. Baseline CK was 137 U/L (normal range 20 to 200 U/L). CK subsequently remained within the normal range until Day 120 when the level was 742 U/L (3.7x ULN). The patient had gone to the gym the day prior. On Day 150 (b) (6) CK was normal at 135 U/L. The participant also experienced an AE of muscle spasms (reported term for the AE was “hand cramps”) on Day 77 (b) (6) 14 days after the participant’s third dose of nedosiran (see details above). The duration of the AE was not reported. As noted, the participant’s CK was normal when assessed on Days 45, 60, and 90.
- The participant subsequently rolled over to DCR-PHXC-301 where his baseline CK was normal at 151 U/L on (b) (6). On Day 30, CK was elevated to 2464 U/L (12x ULN). It is not clear if this episode was associated with exercise. Repeat CK on Day 90 was 242 U/L and on Day 120, 107 U/L. Nedosiran dosing was not interrupted or discontinued.

L412: ## **Antidrug Antibody (Immunogenicity)**
L413: The Office of Biotechnology Products reviewed the Applicant's ADA assay, and confirmed that it is suitable to detect ADAs against nedosiran. No participant had treatment-emergent ADAs to nedosiran. One participant had a positive ADA at screening before receiving nedosiran; the titer of the ADA decreased in the patient during the study.
L414: ## **Low Neutrophil Counts**
L415: Four participants (17%) in the nedosiran group and none in the placebo group had low neutrophil counts of &lt;500 cells/\muL for a risk difference of 17% (95% CI 2, 33). All participants (b) (6) were from the same study site (101) and all episodes were transient and resolved despite continuation of nedosiran. None of the participants experienced infections during the time that the low neutrophil counts were reported. Given the findings at Site 101, the Applicant split samples from three participants for evaluation at both a local and central laboratory. According to the Applicant, neutrophil counts were within normal limits for all samples analyzed at the local laboratory, and were reported to be low for all samples analyzed at the central laboratory. The Applicant hypothesizes that "transportation issues may have affected the quality of the specimens, as literature describes issues with collection (i.e., cold tubes) and transport (i.e., taking too long to be analyzed) as a possible cause of low ANC (Brigden and Dalal 1999; Nagler et al. 2014)."
L416: ## **7.6.2.7. Assessment of Drug-Induced Liver Injury,
## DCR-PHXC-201**
L417: Figure 5 shows a screening assessment for potential cases of drug-induced liver injury. Most participants in both groups maintained ALT, AST, and total bilirubin values within the normal range during the trial. No patient had an AST or ALT value &gt;3x ULN, or total bilirubin &gt;2x ULN.
L418: **Figure 5. Hepatocellular Drug-Induced Liver Injury Screening Plot, Safety Population, DCR-PHXC-201**
L419: Source: ad b.xpt; software: R.
L420: Each data point represents a patient plotted by their maximum ALT or AST versus their maximum total bilirubin values in the postbaseline period.
L421: A potential Hy's Law case (red circle) was defined as having any postbaseline total bilirubin equal to or exceeding 2x ULN within 30 days after a postbaseline ALT or AST equal to or exceeding 3x ULN, and ALP less than 2x ULN (note ALP values are not circled). All patients with at least one postbaseline ALT or AST and bilirubin are plotted.
L422: Abbreviations: ALP, alkaline phosphatase; ALT, alanine aminotransferase; AST, aspartate aminotransferase; DILI, drug-induced liver injury; TB, total bilirubin; ULN, upper limit of normal
L423: ## 7.6.2.8. Vital Signs' Analyses, DCR-PHXC-201
L424: There were no significant treatment-emergent changes from baseline in blood pressure, heart rate, or respiratory rate in either treatment group, or significant differences between the treatment groups.
L425: ## 7.6.2.9. Subgroup Analyses, DCR-PHXC-201
L426: Review of the safety data in the subset of participants with PH1 did not identify additional safety concerns (additional information on safety data in patients with PH1 in Section 17). Overall, the safety data in the overall trial population was driven by the findings in participants with PH1. Subgroup analyses based on demographic factors were not conducted given the size of the study population and the safety profile seen in the larger study population.
L427: ## 7.6.3. Safety Results, DCR-PHXC-301
L428: As shown in Table 32, of the 51 participants enrolled in Study DCR-PHXC-301, twelve participants were on placebo prior to enrolling in Study DCR-PHXC-301. Among the 51 participants enrolled in the study, the median duration of exposure to nedosiran (not considering
L429: the exposure in DCR-PHXC-201 prior to enrollment in DCR-PHXC-301) was 11 months (range 1 to 29 months). Eighteen participants (35%) received nedosiran for 18 months or longer.

L435: ## 7.6.3.1. Overview of Treatment-Emergent Adverse Events
## Summary, DCR-PHXC-301
L436: Overall, 47 participants (92%) experienced a TEAE during Study DCR-PHXC-301, most of which were mild (Table 33). Four participants (8%), all previously on nedosiran in Study DCR-PHXC-201 experienced SAEs, and three participants (6%), all previously on nedosiran in Study DCR-PHXC-201, interrupted dosing because of an AE. No participant discontinued nedosiran because of AEs. There were no deaths.

L444: ## 7.6.3.2. Deaths, DCR-PHXC-301
L445: There were no deaths in Study DCR-PHXC-301 as of the data cutoff.
L446: ## 7.6.3.3. Serious Treatment-Emergent Adverse Events, DCR-PHXC-301
L447: Four participants (8%) experienced SAEs in Study DCR-PHXC-301 (Table 34.). These SAEs included abdominal pain, pyelonephritis, pain in extremity, acute kidney injury, calculus urinary, and nephrolithiasis. The SAEs of pyelonephritis, acute kidney injury, calculus urinary, and nephrolithiasis are events that are commonly seen in patients with PH and are not discussed further. The SAEs of abdominal pain and pain in the extremity were likely not caused by nedosiran as indicated in the narratives below.
L448: - *Participant* (b)(6): A 37-year-old male with PH1 was admitted for one day for transient epigastric abdominal pain 16 days after the second dose of nedosiran. The pain resolved after a few hours and did not lead to interruption or discontinuation of nedosiran.
- *Participant* (b)(6): A 26-year-old female with PH1 was admitted for an SAE of pain in extremity because of infiltration of an intravenous infusion.

L456: ## 7.6.3.4. Adverse Events and FDA Medical Queries Leading to Treatment Discontinuation or Dose Interruptions, DCR-PHXC-301
L457: No participants discontinued study treatment because of AEs.
L458: ## Dose Interruptions
L459: The dose of nedosiran was interrupted in three participants (6%) because of AEs, including in one participant because of hospitalization for SAEs of nephrolithiasis and pyelonephritis (b) (6), one participant for elevated AST, ALT, and bilirubin (b) (6), and one patient because of COVID-19 (b) (6). All the AEs resolved, and all patients continued to receive nedosiran treatment as of data cut-off.
L460: - *Participant* (b) (6): A 28-year-old male with a history of PH1, nephrolithotomies, and ureteroscopy with stent insertion, on concomitant magnesium and potassium citrate. The participant received a single dose of nedosiran 3 mg/kg on (b) (6), in Study DCR-PHXC-101. He received his first dose of nedosiran in Study DCR-PHXC-301 on (b) (6) (Day 1). On Day 55 (25 days after the second dose of nedosiran in Study DCR-PHXC-301), the participant was admitted to the hospital with SAEs of nephrolithiasis and pyelonephritis (symptoms included right flank pain, fever, right lower back pain, blood in urine and renal colic). The participant was found to have a large right uretero-pelvic junction kidney stone requiring nephrostomy to relieve obstruction, and *Staphylococcus aureus* bacteremia and pyelonephritis. The infection was treated with intravenous meropenem and vancomycin. The participant missed the third nedosiran dose (Day 60), which was due while he was being treated in the hospital. Nedosiran dosing was resumed on Day 90, after discharge from the hospital. The participant remains on nedosiran as of data cutoff. No additional delays or dosing interruptions occurred as of data cutoff.
L461: - **Participant** (b) (6): A 21-year-old male with history of PH1, stage 2 to 3 CKD, hypertension, vitamin D deficiency, Gilbert syndrome, and nonalcoholic steatohepatitis. The participant was on concomitant citric acid, potassium bicarbonate, sodium citrate, cholecalciferol, magnesium aspartate and pyridoxine. The participant received one dose of nedosiran 6 mg/kg on (b) (6) in Study DCR-PHXC-101. The participant's baseline ALT, AST, total bilirubin, and direct bilirubin were 60 U/L (reference range 13 to 67 U/L), 30 U/L (reference range 16 to 45 U/L), 2.2 mg/dL (reference range 0.3 to 2 mg/dL), and 0.6 mg/dL (reference range 0.05 to 0.5 mg/dL), respectively. On Day 2 of Study DCR-PHXC-101, the participant had a TEAE of blood bilirubin increased (total bilirubin 2.3 mg/dL) that resolved on Day 8 (total bilirubin 1.8 mg/dL). On Day 29 of DCR-PHXC-101, the participant experienced TEAEs of alanine aminotransferase increased (ALT 86 U/L) and blood bilirubin increased (total bilirubin 2.1 mg/dL, direct bilirubin lower than baseline at 0.5 mg/dL). The blood bilirubin increased resolved on Day 46 and the 'alanine aminotransferase increased' was ongoing at the end of the study. At the end of the study (Day 57), the participant's ALT was 106 U/L, AST 48 U/L, total bilirubin 1.8 mg/dL, and direct bilirubin was 0.5 mg/dL.

- The participant began monthly dosing with nedosiran in Study DCR-PHXC-301 on (b) (6) (Day 1). At Baseline in Study DCR-PHXC-301, ALT was 72 U/L (reference 0 to 45 U/L), AST 52 U/L (reference 0 to 41 U/L), total bilirubin 1.3 mg/dL (reference 0 to 1.2 mg/dL), direct bilirubin 0.41 mg/dL, and alkaline phosphatase (ALP) was 70 U/L. On Day 2, AEs of AST increase and blood bilirubin increased were reported. On Day 2, liver assessments included ALT of 101 U/L, AST 54 U/L, total bilirubin 1.6 mg/dL, direct bilirubin 0.6 mg/dL, and ALP of 63 U/L. The participant remained on nedosiran; ALT fluctuated from 94 to 99 U/L, AST ~45 U/L, and total bilirubin 1.1 to 1.6 mg/dL. On Day 45 (b) (6) AEs of aminotransferase increased [worsening of AST elevation], alanine aminotransferase increased [worsening of ALT elevation], and blood bilirubin increased [worsening of bilirubin elevation] were reported. Evaluation of the participant's liver assessments revealed an increase in ALT to a maximum of 156 U/L on Day 48, ranging from 143 to 98 U/L thereafter; the last available ALT was 115 U/L at Month 21. AST was 57 U/L on Day 45, varied between 57 to 48 U/L from Day 48 to Month 9, increased transiently to 70 U/L at Month 12, then decreased to 47 U/L at Month 15; the last AST was 49 U/L at Month 21. Total bilirubin peaked at 2.1 mg/dL on Day 28 then decreased to 1.4 mg/dL on Day 30 and ranged from 1.3 to 1.9 mg/dL thereafter; the last available result at Month 21 was 1.8 mg/dL.

- The participant's ALT was &gt;3x ULN on Day 45, Month 6, and Month 12 (156 U/L, 138 U/L, and 143 U/L, respectively) and bilirubin was &gt;1.5x ULN (ULN 1.2 mg/dL) at Month 4 (1.9 mg/dL). The patient did not meet criteria for nedosiran discontinuation (ALT or AST &gt;5x baseline, with baseline &lt;2x ULN); however, nedosiran dosing was delayed while an evaluation of the elevations on Day 45 was undertaken.

- The participant was evaluated by a gastroenterologist on (b) (6) (~Month 19) who concluded based on additional investigation including elastography, that the participant had nonalcoholic fatty liver disease.

- This participant's fluctuations in liver assessments were likely secondary to other factors including Gilbert syndrome and nonalcoholic fatty liver disease. A causal relationship with nedosiran is unlikely given that the liver assessments did not continue to worsen while the participant remained on nedosiran for 21 months in Study DCR-PHXC-301.
L462: - *Participant* (b) (6): A 45-year-old male with PH2. Nedosiran dosing was temporarily interrupted when the participant developed COVID-19 at Month 17. Dosing was resumed at Month 18 and the participant remained on nedosiran as of data cutoff.

- *Participant* (b) (6): A 22-year-old female with a history of PH1, nephrolithiasis, CKD, musculoskeletal chest pain, and vitamin D deficiency. At Month 21, the patient developed TEAEs of alkaline phosphatase increased, alanine aminotransferase increased, aspartate aminotransferase increased, bilirubin conjugated increased, and blood lactate dehydrogenase increased. The AEs were mild and resolved approximately 2 weeks later.

Review of the participant's laboratory assessments revealed that from Month 18 to Month 21, there was an increase of ALT from 24 to 113 U/L, AST from 24 to 103 U/L, LDH from 186 to 337 U/L, and ALP from 89 to 117 U/L. Total bilirubin increased from 0.7 mg/dL at Month 15 to 1 mg/dL at Month 18, then decreased to 0.5 mg/dL at Month 21. When rechecked approximately 2 weeks later (last available assessments), AST was 36 U/L, ALT 34 U/L, bilirubin 0.6 mg/dL, LDH 199 U/L, and ALP 91 U/L.

- Nedosiran dosing was not interrupted or delayed and the patient remained on nedosiran as of data cutoff.

- *Participant* (b) (6): A 30-year-old male with a history of PH1, kidney stones, obesity, dizziness, tendonitis, and Hashimoto thyroiditis. The participant was on concomitant levothyroxine, sodium bicarbonate, and pyridoxine. The participant was on placebo in Study DCR-PHXC-201 and began treatment with nedosiran in Study DCR-PHXC-301 on (b) (6) (Day 1). At Month 3, the participant had an AE of ALT increased. Review of the participant's liver assessments revealed that the baseline ALT was 29 U/L, increased to 53 U/L at Month 3, then decreased to 45 U/L at Month 6 and 32 U/L at Month 9 (last available assessment as of data cutoff). There were no significant changes from Baseline to Month 3 in AST or bilirubin.

The AE was reported to be mild, nedosiran dosing was not interrupted, and the AE was reported as resolved at Month 6.

- *Participant* (b) (6): A 27-year-old female with a history of PH2, lithotripsy, hypertonia, arthritis, and hypertension. The participant was on concomitant hydrochlorothiazide and ramipril. She received a single dose of nedosiran in Study DCR-PHXC-101 on (b) (6) and began monthly nedosiran in Study DCR-PHXC-301 on (b) (6) (Day 1). Three AEs of aspartate aminotransferase increased were reported on Day 15 (resolved on Day 29), Month 3 (resolved at Month 11), and Month 15 (resolved at Month 18). All TEAEs were considered mild in severity.

The participant was asymptomatic, no new medications were introduced, and liver ultrasound was normal. Evaluation of liver assessments reveals that the patient had an elevated AST at screening of 51 U/L and at Baseline of 45 U/L (reference range
L481: 0 to 41 U/L). On Day 15, AST increased to 73 U/L (maximum level while in the Study), then fluctuated from 42 to 53 U/L from Day 30 to Month 12, increased to 58 U/L at Month 15, and fluctuated from 40 to 51 U/L from Months 18 to 24. There were no significant changes in ALT or bilirubin during this time. Nodosiran dosing was not interrupted or delayed.
L482: ## 7.6.3.6. Laboratory Findings, DCR-PHXC-301
L483: ## Kidney Tests
L484: Three participants (6%) had an increase in serum creatinine to \ge 1.5\times baseline, including one participant (2%) with an elevation \ge 2\times baseline but &lt;3\times baseline. These are expected events in participants with PH, and as noted in Section 7.6.2.6, these laboratory findings occurred with equal or lower frequency in patients on nedosiran compared with participants on placebo in DCR-PHXC-201.

L491: ## Creatine Phosphokinase/Creatine Kinase
L492: As shown in Table 38, 7 participants (14%) had an increase in CPK &gt;3\times ULN, including 4 participants (8%) with levels &gt;5\times ULN, and 2 participants (4%) with levels &gt;10\times ULN. None of the participants had symptoms of muscle pain or weakness, all remained on nedosiran, and CPK levels returned to the normal range without additional intervention in all but one participant ( (b)(6) ; as noted in Section 7.6.2.6, Participant (b)(6) had fluctuating CK levels that could have been related to exercise). The narratives for these participants are provided below.

L499: - **Participant** (b) (6): A 32-year-old female with a history of PH1, lithotripsy, hypertension, and psoriasis. The participant was on concomitant topical betamethasone, desogestrel (oral contraception), doxylamine, and ramipril. She received a single dose of nedosiran 3 mg/kg in Study DCR-PHXC-101 on (b) (6), and began monthly nedosiran in Study DCR-PHXC-301 on (b) (6) (Day 1). The participant's baseline CPK in DCR-PHXC-101 was 2456 U/L (11x ULN; normal range 35 to 228 U/L), and also the highest CPK for this participant. The CPK subsequently down-trended to 1001 U/L on Day 2 and remained normal thereafter. In DCR-PHXC-301, the CPK levels remained within normal (20 to 180 U/L) up to data cutoff, with the exception of a slightly higher CK value of 183 U/L on Day 30.

The participant's CPK was normal throughout DCR-PHXC-101. The participant's baseline CPK in DCR-PHXC-301 was elevated to &gt;13x ULN at 3691 U/L (normal range 2 to 251 U/L) before receiving nedosiran. On Day 2, CPK trended down to 1875 U/L, and subsequently, except for one mildly elevated level of 274 U/L at Month 18, remained within normal from Day 15 to Month 21.

- **Participant** (b) (6): A 26-year-old male with a history of PH2, chronic back and flank pain, and nephrolithiasis. The participant was on concomitant ibuprofen, hydromorphone, and medical marijuana. He received a single dose of nedosiran 3 mg/kg on (b) (6), in Study DCR-PHXC-101 and began monthly nedosiran in Study DCR-PHXC-301 on (b) (6) (Day 1). The participant's baseline CPK was 74 U/L (normal range 20 to 200 U/L) in DCR-PHXC-101. On Day 29, 28 days after the single dose of nedosiran, CPK transiently increased to 686 U/L (3.4x ULN), then remained within the normal range from Day 43 to the end of the study (Day 57).

The participant's baseline CPK in DCR-PHXC-301 was mildly elevated at 368 U/L (normal range 20 to 200 U/L), and despite nedosiran dosing on Day 1, CPK down-trended to 242 U/L on Day 2. All subsequent CPK levels to Month 21 were within the normal range.

L514: ## 8. Therapeutic Individualization
L515: ## 8.1. Intrinsic Factors
L516: ## Renal Impairment
L517: The Applicant has proposed [REDACTED] (b) (4) as shown in Table 39, and no dose adjustment for patients with mild or moderate renal impairment. The Applicant also proposed to [REDACTED] (b) (4) (b) (4)
L518: Table 39.
L519: Renal Impairment	PK Parameters	Normal	Mild	Moderate
Normal	Cmax	100%	100%	100%
Normal	AUC0-last	100%	100%	100%
Mild	Cmax	100%	100%	100%
Mild	AUC0-last	100%	100%	100%
Moderate	Cmax	100%	100%	100%
Moderate	AUC0-last	100%	100%	100%
L520: Studies DCR-PHXC-101 and DCR-PHXC-201 enrolled participants with normal renal function as well as those with mild to moderate renal impairment. Of 18 participants with PH1 enrolled in DCR-PHXC-101, 10, and 3 participants had mild and moderate renal impairment, respectively. The differences in exposure (Cmax, AUC0-last) between participants with renal impairment and those with normal renal function were dose dependent; no PK differences were observed among participants with normal renal impairment and those with mild-to-moderate renal impairment.
L521: Of the 23 participants (PH1 and PH2) randomized to active treatment in DCR-PHXC-201, 12, 7, and 4 participants had normal, mild, and moderate renal impairment, respectively. Two participants with moderate renal impairment were adolescents; all other participants with renal impairment were adults. PK sampling for nedosiran in DCR-PHXC-201 was conducted on
L522: Days 1, 30 and 150, and did not extend beyond 24 h (Days 1, 30) or 12 h (Day 150). No differences in the PK profiles were observed between participants with normal renal function and those with mild to moderate renal impairment during this initial 12 to 24 h (C_{max}) sampling period (see Section 14.2); however, the complete elimination profile (AUC_{0-last}) was not obtained. Given the small number of participants enrolled in the study and incomplete PK sampling, the observed total AUCs could not be calculated; hence, population PK/PD predictions were used to assess the impact of renal impairment on PK and PD. Based on the population PK/PD predictions, mild and moderate renal impairment is not expected to have a clinically significant effect on the PK or the PD (24 h urinary oxalate excretion) of nedosiran (see Section 14.5).
L523: PH patients with severe renal impairment (an eGFR &lt;30 mL/min/1.73 m^2) and those with kidney failure being treated with dialysis were not enrolled in the trials that were submitted to support the marketing application.
L524: The review team agrees that labeling should indicate that no dose adjustment is needed in patients with (b) (4) (\ge 30 mL/min/1.73 m^2).
L525: ## Hepatic Impairment
L526: The Applicant has proposed the following language for use of nedosiran in patients with hepatic impairment (HI):
L527: No dose adjustment of nedosiran is recommended for patients with mild hepatic impairment (total bilirubin \le upper limit of normal (ULN) and aspartate aminotransferase [AST] &gt; ULN or total bilirubin &gt;1 to 1.5 times ULN and any AST). Nedosiran has not been studied in patients with moderate or severe hepatic impairment (total bilirubin &gt;1.5 ULN with any AST).
L528: Trials for nedosiran enrolled five participants with mild HI based on the National Cancer Institute Organ Dysfunction Working Group (NCI-ODWG) criteria. Two patients were from the single dose study (DCR-PHXC-101) and three patients were from multiple dose studies (DCR-PHXC-201 and DCR-PHXC-301). No patients with moderate or severe HI were enrolled in the studies. The nedosiran concentration-time and 24 h urinary oxalate-time profiles were comparable between the normal hepatic function group and mild HI group (see Section 14.2) and HI status (normal versus mild) was not a significant covariate in the population PK/PD model of nedosiran.
L529: The review team agrees with the proposed labeling that no dose adjustment of nedosiran is required in patients with mild HI and that nedosiran has not been studied in patients with moderate or severe HI (total bilirubin &gt;1.5 ULN with any AST) (see Section 14.2).
L530: ## **Body Weight**
L531: The proposed dosing regimen includes a body weight band-based dosing regimen in adults and adolescents (160 mg once monthly if age 12 years or above with weight \ge50 kg, 128 mg once monthly if age 12 years or above with weight &lt;50 kg) (b) (4)
L532: Body weight was incorporated in the PK model by scaling the clearance and volume parameters allometrically. Body weight was not a significant covariate for the PD model. For an overview of the results supporting the proposed dosing regimen see Section 14.5. The review team recommends including a dose cap of 128 mg for pediatric patients 9 to &lt;12 years of age with body weight &lt;50 kg, to prevent a potential decrease in dose when 11-year-old patients turn 12 years of age (see Section 6.1). This approach does not compromise efficacy in this age and weight group.
L533: ## **Other Intrinsic Factors**
L534: Based on population PK modeling, age and gender are not significant covariates that influence the PK of nedosiran. Across the studies, the proportion of male patients enrolled ranged from 44 to 47%. In Study DCR-PHXC-103, the PK of nedosiran was similar in healthy Japanese versus Caucasian participants, across the dose range of (1.5 to 6 mg/kg). In addition, race (white/Asian/black/unknown/other) was not found to be a significant covariate in the population PK model.
L535: No dose adjustment is needed for nedosiran based on age, gender, or race.
L536: ## **8.2. Extrinsic Factors**
L537: ## **Drug Interactions**
L538: The drug-drug interaction potential of nedosiran involving cytochrome P450 (CYP) enzymes and human transporter proteins was investigated in vitro. Based on the in vitro evaluation, no clinically relevant drug-drug interactions are expected for nedosiran, and no drug-drug interaction-based dose adjustment is necessary.
L539: ## Nedosiran as a Victim
L540: Nedosiran was stable in human hepatocytes and is not metabolized by CYP/uridine 5'-diphospho-glucuronosyl transferase enzymes. Nedosiran was not found to be a substrate of human multidrug and toxin extrusion (MATE) 1, MATE2-K, ornithine aminotransferase (OAT) 1, OAT3, OATP1B1, OATP1B3, ornithine carbamoyltransferase 1, or ornithine carbamoyltransferase 2 uptake transporters. Nedosiran was not found to be a substrate of human breast cancer resistance protein, multidrug resistance-associated protein 1, bile salt export pump, MRP2, MRP3, MRP4, MATE1 or MATE2-K efflux transporters (see Section 14.1). The concomitant use of vitamin B6 (pyridoxine) was not found to be a statistically significant covariate in the PK model of nedosiran (see Section 14.5), however caution is warranted in interpreting this result given the limited sample size for this analysis (only 8 PH1 patients of a total of 143 participants in the pooled study population were on concomitant pyridoxine).
L541: ## Nedosiran as a Perpetrator
L542: Nedosiran did not inhibit any major CYP enzymes or any of the human uptake or efflux drug transporters at clinically relevant concentrations. No time-dependent inhibition of CYP enzymes or CYP induction potential was identified (see Section 14.1).
L543: ## 8.3. Plans for Pediatric Drug Development
L544: On May 15, 2018, FDA granted nedosiran orphan drug designation (designation request number DRU-2018-6354) for the treatment of PH; therefore, the product is exempt from Pediatric Research Equity Act requirements for the indication. Study DCR-PHXC-201 enrolled participants down to 9 years of age; hence, the initial approval of nedosiran for the treatment of PH1 will be extended to patients down to 9 years of age. Juvenile toxicology data are summarized in Section 7.1.
L545: ## 8.4. Pregnancy, Lactation, and Females/Males of Reproductive Potential
L546: ## Animal Data
L547: Developmental and reproductive toxicity studies supporting labeling are summarized below.
L548: ## Nonclinical Data Supporting Labeling on Fertility, Pregnancy and Lactation
L549: **Table 40. Nonclinical Data on Fertility, Pregnancy, and Lactation**
L550: Labeling Section	Nonclinical Data
8.1 Pregnancy	
In mice, subcutaneous administration of nedosiran at doses up to 2000 mg/kg (approximately 58-fold the MRHD based on body surface area) or a mouse-specific (pharmacologically active) analog (10 mg/kg/dose) during organogenesis (dosing on GD 6, 8, 10, 12, and 14 for nedosiran; GD 3 and 10 for the analog) did not have adverse effects on embryo-fetal development.
Subcutaneous administration of nedosiran (0, 2, 6 or 20 mg/kg/dose based on BSA) to pregnant New Zealand White rabbits during organogenesis (dosing on GD 7, 9, 11, 13, 15, 17, and 19) resulted in maternal toxicity on the basis of body weight loss up to 6.5% following the first dose in the 6 and 20 mg/kg/dose groups. Higher postimplantation loss and lower numbers of live fetuses occurred at \ge 6 mg/kg/dose (exposures equivalent to the MRHD based on BSA), and fetal cardiovascular and skeletal malformations occurred at the 20 mg/kg/dose (2-fold the MRHD based on BSA). At the 2 mg/kg/dose, which is below the MRHD, no adverse findings were seen.
In a pre- and postnatal study in mice, subcutaneous administration of nedosiran (0, 250, 500, or 1000 mg/kg/dose) or a mouse-specific (pharmacologically active) analog (10 mg/kg/dose) from implantation to weaning did not have adverse effects on the growth, viability, development, and reproductive performance of the offspring.

8.2 Lactation	No animal studies were conducted to assess placental transfer.
8.3 Females and males of reproductive potential	See animal studies in Labeling Section 13.1, below.
13.1 Fertility	Weekly subcutaneous administration of nedosiran at doses of 500, 1000, or 2000 mg/kg or of a mouse-specific analog (pharmacologically active) at a dose of 10 mg/kg to male mice for 4 weeks prior to and throughout mating, and to female mice for 2 weeks prior to and throughout mating and to GD 7 did not affect male or female fertility or early embryonic development.
L551: Source: Applicant's Nonclinical Study Report
L552: Abbreviations: BSA, body surface area; GD, gestational day; MRHD, minimum recommended human dose
L553: ## Clinical Experience
L554: One pregnancy was reported in Study DCR-PHXC-301. The participant received nedosiran from [REDACTED](b) (6), to [REDACTED](b) (6). Screening pregnancy test was negative before the nedosiran dose on [REDACTED](b) (6). On [REDACTED](b) (6), serum beta-human chorionic gonadotropin pregnancy test was positive (checked because of late menses) and nedosiran was discontinued. The patient had a spontaneous abortion at approximately 5 weeks of pregnancy. According to the Division of Pediatric and Maternal Health's consultation by Drs. Katherine Kratz and Miriam Dinatale, the spontaneous abortion was not likely related to nedosiran's effect on the fetus because (1) based on the PK profile, nedosiran would likely not have been present in the plasma by the time of conception; (2) the asialoglycoprotein receptor is not appreciably expressed in the fetal liver; hence, fetal uptake and toxicity is unlikely; and (3) preclinical studies in mice demonstrated that the active mouse analog of nedosiran, DCR-m355a, reduced maternal expression of LDHA but did not change LDHA expression in pup livers and did not cause embryonic toxicity.
L555: There are no human data regarding the use of nedosiran in lactating women. Drs. Kratz and Dinatale note that based on the drug's large molecular size, it is unlikely that nedosiran will accumulate in human milk.
L556: ## 9. Product Quality
L557: ## Approval
L558: The Office of Pharmaceutical Quality has determined that the NDA meets all applicable standards to support the identity, strength, quality, and purity that it purports the drug product to have. As such, the Office of Pharmaceutical Quality recommends approval of this NDA from a quality perspective. A shelf life of 36 months is granted for the drug product in a single-dose 0.5 mL glass vial (80 mg) when stored at 2°C to 8°C. A shelf-life of 30 months is granted for the drug product in a 0.8 mL prefilled syringe (128 mg) and the drug product in a 1.0 mL prefilled syringe (160 mg) when stored at 2°C to 8°C.
L559: ## 9.1. Device or Combination Product Considerations
L560: Two of the drug product presentations, the 0.8 mL prefilled syringe (128 mg) and 1.0 mL prefilled syringe (160 mg), are combination products. The single dose syringe system for both comprises a (b) (4) glass barrel with a (b) (4) staked needle and rigid needle shield, (b) (4) rubber plunger, and carton. The information supporting the combination product components was reviewed by the Office of Pharmaceutical Quality and found to be adequate.
L561: ## 10. Human Subjects Protections/Clinical Site and Other Good Clinical Practice Inspections/Financial Disclosure Review
L562: ## FDA Inspections
L563: FDA requested an inspection of clinical investigator Site #101. The site enrolled the greatest number of participants in Study DCR-PHXC-201 (7 of 35 enrolled participants) and the size of the treatment effect on the primary endpoint in patients with PH1 decreases substantially when this site is removed from the analysis (though the endpoint remains statistically significant). Of note, it appears that the placebo arm did worse at this site compared with other sites. According to the Clinical Inspection Summary by Dr. Tina Chang in the Division of Clinical Compliance Evaluation, Office of Scientific Investigations, the study appears to have been conducted adequately at Site #101, and the data generated by the clinical investigator site appears acceptable.
L564: No inspections were requested based on financial disclosures. Three investigators at one site in (b) (6) reported financial disclosures exceeding (b) (6) for Study DCR-PHXC-101 (collectively the site investigators received (b) (6) in “direct fees” and (b) (6) for “indirect fees” for an investigator-initiated grant for (b) (6). This site and
L565: principal investigator also enrolled participants in Study DCR-PHXC-201 (Study Site (b)(6)). Collectively, Site (b)(6) enrolled one participant who was randomized to placebo in Study DCR-PHXC-201 and removal of this patient from the efficacy analysis did not alter the study results.
L566: ## **Site Audits and Data Quality Assurance**
L567: The Applicant's "quality group" monitored the Study and conducted investigator site audits to ensure the accuracy and reliability of clinical Study data. According to the DCR-PHXC-201 Clinical Study Report, electronic case report forms were completed from source documents. Investigators were responsible for verifying that data entries were accurate and correct. Study monitors verified electronic case report form data entries for accuracy and completeness.
L568: ## **Protocol Deviations**
L569: The Applicant refers to major protocol deviations as "important" deviations. "Important" protocol deviations were defined as those that could potentially affect the safety of the participant, substantially alter risks to the participant, and/or effect the ethics or the integrity of study data; all others were considered "non-important" (i.e., minor).
L570: Eighty-seven major protocol deviations were reported in 30 participants (21 in the nedosiran group and 9 in placebo):
L571: - The most common major protocol deviations were related to efficacy assessments and reported in 20 participants (14 in the nedosiran group, 6 in placebo). These included efficacy assessments not completed or not completed within the protocol-specified timeframe:Twenty-four-hour urine collections for Uox were not collected/recollected, incorrectly collected, or not processed because of improper labeling of the sample for six participants;
- Spot urine collections were not obtained at various timepoints for seven participants;
- The PedsQL questionnaire was not completed for seven participants;
- The PedsQL Family Impact Module was not completed for three participants;
- Kidney ultrasound assessments were not obtained for three participants;
- One participant's fluid intake information was not collected.
- Four participants missed one dose of Study drug (3 in the nedosiran group, 1 in placebo):*Participant* (b)(6): A participant with PH1 randomized to nedosiran missed the Day 90 dose (and the study visit) for reasons related to the COVID-19 pandemic;
- *Participant* (b)(6): A participant with PH1 randomized to nedosiran did not receive Study drug from Day 60 onward. Dosing was discontinued at the recommendation of the Data Safety and Monitoring Committee because of an AE of tachycardia;
- *Participant* (b)(6): A participant with PH1 randomized to nedosiran – the pharmacist threw away one of the kits for this participant on Day 90. This was after the correct dose was prepared for this participant. It is not clear if the patient subsequently received Study drug on/within the Day 90 window;
- *Participant* (b)(6): A participant with PH1, randomized to placebo, the dose was not increased for weight on Day 120.
L572: - Laboratory/endpoint data were not collected or not evaluable in eight participants (six in the nedosiran group, two in placebo):Serum vitamin B6 levels were not collected or not evaluable for seven patients;
- Plasma oxalate was not collected for one patient;
- PK samples were incorrectly collected for one patient.
- The visit window was missed for six participants (five participants in the nedosiran group, one in placebo):Five participants missed a study visit for reasons related to the COVID-19 pandemic;
- One patient missed a study visit because of their work schedule.
- Safety assessments were not collected in five participants (three nedosiran, two placebo):Two participants missed one to two safety assessments related to the COVID-19 pandemic;
- An ECG was not obtained for one participant;
- One participant missed a telemedicine safety assessment;
- One participant missed hematology laboratory assessments for one visit.
- Two participants were not reconsented when appropriate and one participant was consented using an obsolete consent form.
- Six participants had other major protocol deviations (two in the nedosiran group, four in placebo):Kidney ultrasound not anonymized for four participants;
- For one participant, weight and height assessments were obtained verbally from the participant (not measured in clinic);
- For one participant a prescription form was signed by a “nonauthorized” physician.
L573: Of note, all missed 24 h Uox samples were imputed for the primary efficacy analysis, as specified in the Statistical Analysis Plan. No participant missed more than one Uox assessment after Day 90. It is possible that missed doses in the nedosiran group could have led to an attenuation of the treatment effect in those participants; however, despite this the treatment effect in the overall trial population remained significant. It is unlikely that the protocol deviations affected participant safety.
L574: All other protocol deviations were minor.
L575: ## 11. Advisory Committee Summary
L576: The application does not raise significant issues regarding the safety or effectiveness of the drug requiring external expert advice; hence, no Advisory Committee meeting was held.
L577: ## III. Additional Analyses and Information
L578: ## 12. Summary of Regulatory History
L579: ## U.S. Regulatory Actions and Marketing History
L580: Nedosiran is not marketed in the United States, and there have been no previous regulatory actions.
L581: ## Summary of Presubmission/ Submission Regulatory Activity
L582: There were several interactions with FDA over the course of development; a summary of key regulatory milestones, agreements, and advice is provided in Table 41.
L583: **Table 41. Key Regulatory Milestones, Agreements, and Advice**
L584: Date/Source	Advice From Agency/Regulatory Action
December 13, 2016
Pre-IND Meeting	The Division indicated that it may be willing to accept a substantial change in Uox in patients with very high baseline levels as a surrogate endpoint in patients with primary hyperoxaluria and a prior history of kidney stones, but to do so additional data were needed to support Uox as a surrogate endpoint in this disease, e.g., data indicating that treatment effects on Uox reliably predicted treatment effects on clinical outcomes in PH and information on the magnitude of a treatment's effect on Uox that could be considered clinically meaningful.
December 10, 2018
End-of-Phase 1 Meeting	The Division noted that based on the provided data from the Rare Kidney Stone Consortium Registry on the relationship between Uox and progression to kidney failure as well as data on the effects of reduction of Uox on nephrocalcinosis and rate of kidney function decline in patients with PH1 after pre-emptive liver transplantation, a substantial change in Uox could be used as a basis for traditional approval of nedosiran in patients with PH1 and relatively preserved kidney function, no signs of systemic oxalosis, and high baseline Uox levels. The Division requested more information on typical Uox levels in patients with PH2 and patients with PH3, as well as the relationship between Uox levels and clinical manifestations in patients with PH3.
March 19, 2019
Type A Meeting	The Division agreed with the Applicant's proposed primary endpoint for Study DCR-PHXC-201, i.e., measurement of 24 h urinary oxalate burden, expressed as time-weighted standardized (TWS) area under the curve (AUC) from Day 90 to 180, based on percentage change from baseline. However, the Division also indicated that achieving a statistically significant treatment effect would not be sufficient for approval and reiterated that the trial would also need to enroll a sufficient number of patients with markedly elevated Uox levels at baseline and show a substantial reduction in Uox in this population.
July 2, 2019
Breakthrough Therapy Designation	The Agency granted nedosiran Breakthrough Therapy designation for the treatment of PH1 based on marked and sustained reductions in Uox levels in patients with PH1 treated with nedosiran in a Phase 2 study.

L586: Date/Source	Advice From Agency/Regulatory Action
January 26, 2022
Pre-NDA Meeting	
The Applicant requested this meeting to obtain Agency feedback on their plans to address the concerns raised by the Division during the October 21, 2021, meeting.
The Agency indicated that the additional analyses proposed by the Applicant were reasonable to support efficacy in PH1. The Applicant's proposal included:Conducting all prespecified efficacy analyses outlined in the SAP for DCR-PHXC-201 for the primary endpoint including sensitivity and subgroup analyses, and for the secondary endpoints for patients with PH1 and PH2 (i.e., the trial population as a whole), and the subgroup of patients with PH1
Assessing the percentage change in 24 h Uox from baseline to Day 180 (averaged from Day 90 to 180) in patients with PH1
Evaluating the proportion of patients with PH1 with normal 24 h Uox at Day 180

The Agency requested additional analyses including the percent change in 24 h Uox from baseline to Day 180 (averaged from Day 90 to 180) and the proportion of patients with normal 24 h Uox at Day 180 for the trial population as a whole.
The Agency indicated that the Applicant's proposed subgroup, sensitivity, and post hoc analyses to address the impact of the baseline imbalances in 24 h Uox levels and the proportion of patients with high baseline Uox levels between the two study arms were reasonable. These analyses included:The primary endpoint analysis with baseline Uox as a covariate in the ANCOVA model (prespecified primary endpoint analysis).
The primary endpoint analysis in the subgroup of patients with "high" baseline Uox >1600\mumol/24 h in the overall population (prespecified subgroup analysis) and patients with PH1 only.
A post hoc primary endpoint analysis in the subgroup of patients with baseline Uox <1600\mumol/24 h in patients with PH1 (sample size of each treatment group not provided with topline results).
A post hoc primary endpoint analysis of patients with PH1 excluding two placebo patients with 24 h Uox >2800\mumol/24 h (>6x the upper limit of normal and "significantly higher" than the mean for the placebo group).
A post hoc analysis of percentage change from baseline in 24 h Uox in DCRPHXC-301 for patients with PH1 who were previously in the placebo arm in DCR-PHXC-201.

The Agency indicated that the Applicant's proposed analyses of Study DCR-PHXC-301 could serve as confirmatory evidence of effectiveness includingChanges in Uox in patients with PH1 who rolled over from single dose study DCR-PHXC-101 to demonstrate changes whilst on treatment, off-treatment, and on-treatment again
Analyses of the percentage change in 24 h Uox from DCR-PHXC-201 baseline to Month 3 and Month 6 in DCRPHXC-301, based on the available data at data cutoff, for patients with PH1 previously randomized to placebo in DCR-PHXC-201. Additional 6-month Uox data for the remaining patients would be provided at the time of the safety update.

The Agency indicated that the Applicant's proposed analyses to support the durability of the treatment effect were reasonable and requested that the Applicant submit at least 6 months of Uox data for all placebo patients with PH1 from Study DCR-PHXC-201 who had rolled over to receive nedosiran in DCR-PHXC-301 and submit all available Uox data beyond 6 months until data cutoff.
L587: Source: Table generated by the clinical reviewer from a review of Agency communications with the Applicant.
Abbreviations: ANCOVA, analysis of covariance; IND, investigational new drug; NDA, new drug application; PH1/2/3, primary hyperoxaluria type 1/2/3; SAP, statistical analysis plan; Uox, urinary oxalate
L588: ## 13. Pharmacology Toxicology
L589: ## 13.1. Summary Review of Studies Submitted With the Investigational New Drug Application
L590: ## 13.1.1. Pharmacology
L591: ## 13.1.1.1. Primary Pharmacology
L592: - Nedosiran is a dicer-substrate small interfering ribonucleic acid (siRNA) oligonucleotide conjugated to N-acetyl-D-galactosamine (GalNAc) to enable uptake by the asialoglycoprotein receptor, which is highly expressed in the hepatocytes. It is designed to silence the expression of lactate dehydrogenase A (LDHA) messenger ribonucleic acid (mRNA), and thereby reduce production of oxalate in the liver.
- The target sequence of nedosiran in LDHA mRNA is conserved in humans and nonhuman primates, but not in mice, and thus, nedosiran is fully active in cynomolgus monkeys but inactive in mice.
- Administration of nedosiran (1, 3, 6 mg/kg) to a knock-in mouse model expressing human LDHA mRNA resulted in dose-dependent knockdown of LDHA mRNA in the liver that was sustained for 4 weeks postdose.
- A single subcutaneous (SC) dose of nedosiran (1, 3 mg/kg) in cynomolgus monkeys resulted in reduction of LDHA mRNA by 28% and 72%, indicating that a single low dose of 1 mg/kg (human equivalent dose 0.3 mg/kg) is a pharmacologically active dose.
- In a mouse model of PH1 induced by knockout of the alanine-glyoxylate aminotransferase 1 gene, a single SC dose of 0.5, 1, or 5 mg/kg DCR-m355 (the mouse active analog of nedosiran) reduced urinary oxalate concentration (normalized to creatinine); the reduction was sustained for 4 weeks, indicating that suppression of LDHA mRNA correlates with reduction of oxalate to near normal levels.
- In a mouse model of PH2 generated using siRNA-mediated knockdown of the *Grhpr* gene, urinary oxalate concentration was reduced to baseline levels after three weekly doses of DCR-m355 (10 mg/kg), indicating that inhibition of hepatic LDHA mRNA expression reduces oxalate production.
L593: - In a mouse model of PH3 generated by drinking 1% hydroxyproline, SC administration of DCR-m355 at a dose level of 5 mg/kg weekly for 3 weeks prevented a hydroxyproline-induced elevation of creatinine-normalized urinary oxalate concentration, demonstrating the potential of LDHA mRNA knockdown for the treatment of PH.
- In CD-1 mice subjected to running on a treadmill, administration of DCR-m355 (5 mg/kg) once per week for 4 weeks resulted in significant LDHA mRNA knockdown (&gt;95%) in the liver, with no effect on skeletal muscle LDHA mRNA expression or changes in circulating lactate, pyruvate and creatine kinase levels, demonstrating selective inhibition of LDHA mRNA in the liver without alterations of skeletal muscle LDHA isoenzyme activity.
L594: ## 13.1.1.2. Secondary Pharmacology
L595: The potential for off-target activity of nedosiran was assessed via an in silico bioinformatics analysis of the human genome database using basic local alignment tools to identify sequences that may be homologous with the full-length or shortened chain portions of the antisense and sense strands of nedosiran. The in silico analysis showed minimal potential for RNAi-mediated hybridization to nontarget RNAs. The half-maximal inhibitory concentration for nedosiran against its target transcript LDHA is approximately 25pM in an in vitro human cell line assay, which is &gt;10,000-fold lower than the potential for nontarget transcripts, indicating that none of the selected genes with complementarity to the guide strand are likely off-targets for nedosiran. Overall, the sequence of nedosiran has specificity for the intended target LDHA mRNA with low likelihood of hybridization to off-target RNAs.
L596: ## 13.1.1.3. Safety Pharmacology
L597: - Nedosiran did not inhibit human ether-a-go-go related gene channel current up to a concentration of 48 µg/mL.
- Nedosiran had no significant adverse effect on central nervous system, respiratory, or cardiovascular function of cynomolgus monkeys at doses up to 300 mg/kg.

Repeated dose SC injection.	Nedosiran is absorbed rapidly with a T_{max} of 0.5 to 2 h and 1.5 to 8 h in mice and monkeys, respectively. Peak plasma concentration rapidly declined in a multiexponential manner, with an initial rapid distribution phase to tissues, followed by a much slower terminal elimination phase, indicating equilibrium between plasma and tissue concentration. [^{3}H]-Nedosiran mice 5 mg/kg	Nedosiran is distributed primarily to the liver, and to a lesser extent to the kidney. Nedosiran is metabolized by sequential removal of the four GalNAcs and four pentanamido linker moieties in the lysosomes. The presence of all 4 GalNAc ligands in the urine indicate nedosiran that had not been taken up into cells and metabolized, but instead was cleared from systemic circulation directly via renal excretion.

L610: ## 13.1.3. Toxicology
L611: ## 13.1.3.1. General Toxicology
L612: Repeat dose toxicology studies were conducted in mice for up to 26 weeks and monkeys for up to 39 weeks. Key results from these pivotal chronic toxicology studies are summarized in the following subsections. The monkey is considered a pharmacologically relevant model because the LDHA mRNA target sequence has 100% homology with humans. Nedosiran is inactive in the mouse because the LDHA mRNA sequence is different from humans, and hence, the toxicology of nedosiran was studied in the mouse to assess off-target hybridization effects. The animals were dosed once every 4 weeks to mimic the clinical monthly dosing frequency.
L613: ## Study Title: 6-Month Repeat-Dose Study of Nedosiran in Juvenile Mice
L614: ## Key Study Findings:
L615: - In the chronic 6-month study in juvenile mice (Table 44), nedosiran administered SC once every 4 weeks at a dose level of 300 mg/kg was associated with a greater incidence and severity of injection site reactions consisting of mononuclear cell infiltration, fibroplasia, and macrophage hypertrophy which were resolved at the end of the 8-week recovery period (Table 45).
- Macrophagic hypertrophic changes at the injection sites are attributed to accumulation of the test article and are considered a class effect of oligonucleotides.
- The no observable adverse effect level (NOAEL) in the juvenile mouse was considered to be 300 mg/kg, with a corresponding mean area under the concentration-time curve from dosing to the time of the last measured concentration (AUC_{last}) of 526 \mug\cdoth/mL on Day 169, which is approximately 38-fold the exposures at a human dose of 2.8 mg/kg, with a corresponding AUC_{last} of 13.6 \mug\cdoth/mL.

L620: **Table 45. Observations and Results**
L621: Parameter	Major Findings
Mortality	Two animals in the high dose group of 300 mg/kg were euthanized on Day 77 due to declining condition. One animal in the control group was found dead on Day 164. Gross examination showed a mass in the thymus at necropsy, and microscopic examination revealed lymphoma derived from thymic lymphocytes. The rest of the animals survived to scheduled sacrifice.
Clinical signs	No changes in clinical signs were observed.
Body weights	No test article-related changes in body weights were observed.
Clinical chemistry	No changes in hematology, serum chemistry and urinalysis parameters were observed. One animal in the mid-dose group exhibited an increase in liver enzymes (alanine aminotransferase, aspartate aminotransferase) and creatine kinase. Sporadic increases in these enzymes are common in mice and are considered secondary to handling. Minor wounds, abrasions, and hair loss were noted sporadically throughout the study, and were considered incidental.
Gross pathology	No changes were observed during the assessment of gross pathology at terminal or recovery necropsies.
Organ weights	No test article-related changes in organ weights were noted at terminal necropsy.
Histopathology	Test article-related findings at terminal necropsy were limited to the injection sites and consisted of mild subcutaneous mononuclear cell infiltrates, fibroplasia and macrophage hypertrophy in the 300 mg/kg dose group. All findings were resolved by the end of the 8-week recovery period.
Adequate battery: Yes	
Peer reviewed: Yes
L622: Parameter	Major Findings
Toxicokinetics	
On Day 1, maximum plasma concentration (C_{max}) increased in a dose-proportional manner from 30 mg/kg to 100 mg/kg (3.9-fold for a 3.3-fold increase in dose) and 300 mg/kg (9.3-fold for a 10-fold increase in dose). On Day 169, C_{max} was dose proportional at 100 mg/kg (3.2-fold) and less than dose proportional at 300 mg/kg (6.8-fold). Total exposure (AUC_{last}) on Days 1 and 169 was greater than dose proportional over the dose range evaluated (100 mg/kg: 4.8- and 4.6-fold, respectively; 300 mg/kg: 20.5- and 17.2-fold, respectively). Group mean accumulation ratios for C_{max} ranged from 1.34 to 1.85 and for AUC_{last} ranged from 1.68 to 1.99. T_{max} was observed at the first (1 h) or second (2 h) time point postdose. Plasma elimination half-life could not be determined in mice. No sex-related difference in plasma concentration of nedosiran was apparent.

Tissue concentration	
The tissue concentration of nedosiran was higher in the liver than the kidney. The concentration of nedosiran in both tissues increased with increasing dose and was less than dose proportional in the liver and greater than dose proportional in the kidney. Over a 10-fold dose range, mean nedosiran concentration increased 3-fold in the liver and up to 17-fold in the kidney.

L625: ## **Study Title: 39-Week Repeat-Dose Study of Nedosiran in Juvenile and Adult Monkey**
L626: ## **Key Study Findings**
L627: - In the chronic 9-month study in the cynomolgus monkey (Table 46), nedosiran administered SC once every 4 weeks was associated with minimal to moderate basophilic macrophage hypertrophy in the liver Kupffer cells, mesenteric lymph node, and injection sites at doses \ge 30 mg/kg, and in the mandibular lymph nodes and testes at a dose of 300 mg/kg. Microscopy evaluation of the liver revealed an increase in the number and size of clear vacuoles and lysosomes within hepatocytes in the 300 mg/kg dose group, but there was no degeneration or necrosis (Table 47).

- During the 8-week recovery period, the findings of macrophage basophilic hypertrophy were partially reversed at the injection site; however, the findings in the liver, lymph nodes and testes were still evident in the 300 mg/kg dose group.

- Hypertrophic, basophilic changes in the Kupffer cell/macrophage in the liver and lymph nodes could be due to accumulation of the test article and have been reported with this class of oligonucleotides.

- Findings of basophilic hypertrophy of macrophages in sexually mature monkeys may not suggest adverse effects on fertility because in a dedicated study on fertility and embryonic development study in male mice, there were no nedosiran- or DCR-m355a-related effects on mating and fertility parameters, sperm motility, sperm density, or sperm morphology, reproductive organ weights (testes, epididymides, prostate, and seminal vesicles), and no macroscopic observations were detected at necropsy examination.

- In the juvenile monkey, the histological findings in the liver, lymph nodes and testes were still evident in the 300 mg/kg dose group, and hence, the NOAEL for nedosiran was considered to be 100 mg/kg, with corresponding AUC_{0-48h} 400 \mu\text{g}\cdot\text{h}/\text{mL}, which is
L628: approximately 29-fold the exposures in humans dosed 2.8 mg/kg with a corresponding AUC_{last} 13.6 \mu\text{g}\cdot\text{h}/\text{mL}.

L633: **Table 47. Observations and Results**
L634: Parameter	Major Findings
Mortality	All animals survived to scheduled sacrifice.
Clinical signs	No changes were observed in clinical signs, physical examinations, ECG, and ophthalmology.
Body weights	No changes in body weights were observed.
Injection site observations	Incidental and procedural findings included pink discoloration and/or scab/crust at the injection site but did not increase in severity after repeated dosing.
Hematology	No changes were observed in clinical pathology parameters (including hematology, coagulation, and urinalysis).
Clinical chemistry	No effect on platelet count or mean platelet volume was observed. Sporadic increases in ALT, AST, CK, and LDH were not considered test article related due to their small magnitude and were attributed to handling and restraint.
Urinalysis	Analysis of urine revealed detectable levels of nedosiran and metabolites 48 h postdose on Days 3, 87, and 255 in animals receiving 100 or 300 mg/kg. Among these, intact full-length antisense strand, and sense strands with all four GalNAc groups intact were the most abundant.
Cytokine analysis	No test article-related effect on cytokine activation was evident at any time point evaluated. Cytokine levels, such as G-CSF, IFN-\alpha2a, IFN-\gamma, IL-1\beta, IL-6, IL-8, and TNF-\alpha, were low to undetectable.
Complement analysis	No test article-related effect on complement activation (Bb or C3a) was evident at any time point evaluated.
Gross pathology	No changes attributed to administration of nedosiran were seen in gross pathology at terminal or recovery necropsies.
Organ weights	No test article-related organ weight changes were observed at terminal or recovery necropsies in the juvenile or young adult animals.
L635: Parameter	Major Findings
Histopathology
Adequate battery: Yes
Peer reviewed: Yes	At terminal necropsy, increases in the incidence and/or severity of the Kupffer cell basophilic hypertrophy and hepatocyte basophilia in the liver and macrophage basophilic hypertrophy in the mesenteric lymph node were noted in both the juvenile and young-adult animals. Minimal to mild basophilic hypertrophy of the Kupffer cells/macrophages in the liver, mesenteric lymph node, and injection sites was noted at doses \ge 30 mg/kg. Additional findings included minimal hepatocyte hypertrophy and mixed inflammatory cell infiltration in the liver (juvenile monkey), and macrophage basophilic hypertrophy in the mandibular lymph nodes and testes in the 300 mg/kg dose group. Transmission electron microscopy revealed an increase in the number and size of lysosomes, which contained amorphous electron-dense material, which were not consistent with phospholipidosis. The absence of degeneration or necrosis, or effects on other features of the cell and subcellular organelles, suggests that the observed changes were not adverse. At recovery necropsy, macrophage basophilic hypertrophy was still evident in the liver, lymph nodes, and testes in juveniles dosed with 100 and 300 mg/kg and in young adults dosed with 300 mg/kg, whereas the findings at the injection sites and mixed inflammatory cell infiltrates in the liver were resolved.
Toxicokinetics	C_{\text{max}} increased in an approximately dose-proportional manner with a 10-fold increase in dose resulting in a 9.0-fold and 9.3-fold increase in C_{\text{max}} on Days 1 and 253, respectively. AUC_{0-48} increased in a greater than dose-proportional manner, i.e., a 10-fold increase in dose from 30 to 300 mg/kg resulted in 24- and 23-fold increases in AUC_{0-48} on Days 1 and 253, respectively. Accumulation ratios were similar across dose levels and ranged from 0.993 to 1.29 for C_{\text{max}} and from 1.25 to 1.44 for AUC_{0-48}. Accumulation was similar in juvenile and young adult animals at the 300 mg/kg dose, with AUC_{0-48} accumulation ratios of 1.25 and 1.36, respectively. T_{\text{max}} values ranged from 1.8 to 11 h and tended to increase with dose. The plasma elimination half-life was approximately 4 h. No remarkable sex-related differences in exposure were observed on Day 1 or 253. No age-related differences in toxicokinetics were evident.
Tissue concentration	The tissue concentration of nedosiran increased with dose, although the increases were less than dose proportional. Concentration of nedosiran at terminal sacrifice (Day 255) were higher in the liver than in the kidney. On Day 309 (8 weeks after the last dose), the tissue concentrations of nedosiran were 40.0 to 77.8% (liver) and 7.35 to 16.0% (kidney) of those on Day 255, with the apparent rate of decrease more rapid in the kidney than in the liver.
Pharmacodynamic analysis	By Day 255, nedosiran induced significant reduction in LDHA mRNA expression (92% reduction), and this reduction persisted for at least 8 weeks post-dose (Day 309), with no recovery of LDHA mRNA expression. No age-related difference in the concentration of RNA-induced silencing complex-loaded nedosiran was observed between juvenile and young-adult animals at the 300 mg/kg dose level.

L644: ## 13.1.3.3. Carcinogenicity
L645: The carcinogenic potential of nedosiran has not been assessed. The Agency agrees that carcinogenicity studies can be conducted postmarketing. The available data do not raise concern for a risk of carcinogenicity that should delay approval given the serious nature of the disease and limited therapeutic options.
L646: ## 13.2. Individual Reviews of Studies Submitted With the New Drug Application
L647: ## 13.2.1. Reproductive Toxicology
L648: ## 13.2.1.1. Fertility and Early Embryonic Development
L649: ## **Study Number/Title:** 19-DEV-001/Fertility and Early Embryonic Development Study in Mice
L650: Good laboratory practices (GLP) compliance: Yes
L651: ## Key Study Findings
L652: - In the male fertility study in mice (Table 49), there were no nedosiran- or DCR-m355a-related effects on any mating and fertility parameter, sperm motility, sperm density, sperm morphology, or reproductive organ weights (testes, epididymides, prostate, and seminal vesicles), and no macroscopic observations were detected at necropsy examination.
- In female mice, there were no nedosiran- or DCR-m355a-related effects on estrous cycling, mating, fertility, or ovarian or uterine parameters, and there were no macroscopic observations.
- The NOAEL for fertility and early embryonic development for nedosiran was 2000 mg/kg (about 58-fold the maximum recommended human dose (MRHD) based on body surface area) (Table 50).

Female mice were dosed once weekly beginning 15 days before cohabitation, during cohabitation, and continuing until GD 7 (4 doses).
Deviation from study protocol affecting interpretation of results	There were no deviations that affected the overall integrity of the study or the interpretation of the study results and conclusions.

L657: **Table 50. Observations and Results**
L658: Parameter	Major Findings

Clinical signs	There were no nedosiran or DCR-m355a-related clinical observations.
Body weights	
There was no effect on body weight gains in males and females treated with nedosiran. Administration of DCR-355a resulted in decreased body weight gains in males and females during study Days 1 to 8; thereafter, mean body weight gains were similar to or higher than controls during the premating dose period.
There were no nedosiran or DCR-m355a-related effects on mean maternal body weight gains, mean maternal body weights, or mean gravid uterine weights in females during the gestation period.

Fertility parameters

In females, there were no nedosiran- or DCR-m355a-related effects on estrous cycling, mating, fertility, or ovarian or uterine parameters, and there were no macroscopic observations.

Necropsy	There were no nedosiran or DCR-m355a-related macroscopic observations detected at necropsy examination. There were no nedosiran or DCR-m355a-related effects on terminal body weights or the absolute or relative (to terminal body weight) weight of the testes, epididymides, seminal vesicles or prostate gland.

L672: ## **Study Number/ Title: 19-L1360-L-018 / Embryo-Fetal Development Study in Rabbit**
L673: GLP compliance: Yes
L674: ## **Key Study Findings**
L675: - In an embryofetal development study in New Zealand White rabbits (Table 53), subcutaneous administration of nedosiran on gestational day (GD) 7, 9, 11, 13, 15, 17, and 19 at doses of 2, 6, or 20 mg/kg/dose resulted in maternal toxicity in the form of body weight loss and lower food consumption in the 6 and 20 mg/kg/dose groups following administration of the first dose on GD 8. Thereafter, absolute body weight and body weight gains were comparable to controls beginning on GD 9 to the end of dosing (GD 20). Higher post-implantation loss and lower numbers of live fetuses occurred at \ge 6 mg/kg/dose (0.7x the MRHD), and fetal cardiovascular and skeletal malformations occurred at the 20 mg/kg/dose (2x the MRHD). A dose level of 2 mg/kg/dose (0.2x the MRHD) was considered to be the NOAEL for maternal toxicity and for embryo/fetal development in rabbits. Based on exposures, a dose level of 2 mg/kg in rabbits achieved AUClast 1220 h\cdotng/mL at GD 19, which is approximately 0.1x the human exposure (AUC 13,600 h\cdotng/mL) at the MRHD (Table 54).
- Nedosiran administration did not reduce LDHA mRNA expression in maternal or fetal liver, and thus, it is not pharmacologically active in rabbits. The cause for the embryo-fetal toxicities observed in the rabbit is unclear, but it may be related to the inhibition of citrate synthase mRNA expression, which is the last step in the Krebs cycle ATP generation and is crucial for organ development.
- Similar findings of maternal toxicity and post-implantation loss were observed with a scrambled sequence of chemically comparable GalNAc-conjugated siRNA, indicating that the embryo-fetal toxicity observed in the rabbit may be associated with the bulk chemical and physical properties (e.g., high molecular weight, polyanionic polymer) of the molecule and/or extrahepatic silencing of the mRNA.
L676: **Table 53. Methods Used in the Embryo-Fetal Development Study in Rabbit**
L677: Parameter	Method Details
Dose and frequency of dosing	Nedosiran 2, 6, 20 mg/kg, single injection during organogenesis on GD 7, 9, 11, 13, 15, 17, and 19
Route of administration	Subcutaneous injection
Formulation/vehicle	0.9% sterile sodium chloride for injection, USP
Species/strain	Pregnant New Zealand White rabbits
Number/sex/group	25/dose group
Satellite groups:	3/dose group toxicokinetics
Study design	Female rabbits were administered nedosiran 0, 2, 6 or 20 mg/kg/dose subcutaneously on GD 7, 9, 11, 13, 15, 17, and 19.
Deviation from study protocol affecting interpretation of results	None of the deviations were considered to have impacted the overall integrity of the study or the interpretation of the study results and conclusions.
L678: Source: Applicant's Nonclinical Study Report.
L679: Abbreviations: GD, gestational day; USP, United States Pharmacopeia
L680: **Table 54. Observations and Results**
L681: Parameter	Major Findings
Mortality	All animals survived to the scheduled necropsy on GD 29.
Clinical signs	No nedosiran-related clinical observations were noted at any dose level.
Body weights	Following the first dose (GD 7-8), nedosiran-related mean maternal body weight losses of up to 6.5% and 6.1% compared to the control group were noted in the 6 and 20 mg/kg dose groups, respectively. Mean body weight gains in the 6 and 20 mg/kg dose groups remained higher than the control group during GD 10-11, and thereafter, mean body weight gains were generally comparable to the control group. In addition, mean absolute body weights in the 6 and 20 mg/kg/dose groups were comparable to the control group beginning on GD 9 and continuing to the end of the dosing period (GD 20).
Necropsy findings:
Cesarean section data	At the scheduled necropsy on GD 29, no nedosiran-related internal findings were observed at any dose level.
Necropsy findings:
Offspring	Low dose 2 mg/kg: No nedosiran-related developmental variations were noted at 2 mg/kg.
Mid dose 6 mg/kg: Nedosiran-related higher mean litter proportions of post-implantation loss (primarily early resorptions) were noted at doses \ge6 mg/kg, resulting in lower mean numbers of live fetuses.
High dose 20 mg/kg: Nedosiran-related lower mean gravid uterine weight was noted in the 20 mg/kg/dose group. Nedosiran-related external, visceral, and skeletal fetal malformations were noted in the 20 mg/kg/dose group. Three fetuses had a short or thread-like tail with corresponding skeletal malformations of absent caudal and/or sacral vertebrae. Three different fetuses had a malpositioned posterior vena cava. In addition, one fetus had an overriding aorta and pulmonary trunk atresia, and two fetuses had caudally malpositioned right kidneys. Three fetuses (one of which had a short tail) also had fused thoracic centra.
Toxicokinetics	Peak plasma concentration of nedosiran were observed at 0.5 to 4 h postdose (increasing with dose level) on GD 7 and GD 19. At 48 h postdose, nedosiran concentrations were quantifiable in maternal kidney and liver, fetal plasma, and placenta at all dose levels, and in amniotic fluid (1 litter/dose group) at doses \ge6 mg/kg, but not in fetal kidney or liver. Concentrations were highest in maternal liver followed by maternal kidney. Placenta concentration was 24- to 54-fold higher than maternal plasma concentration. Mean fetal plasma concentration was 2- to 8-fold lower than maternal plasma concentration.
L682: Parameter	Major Findings
Effects on citrate synthase mRNA expression	In silico complementarity analysis indicated that nedosiran may knockdown rabbit citrate synthase mRNA, and hence, maternal and fetal liver tissues were analyzed for mRNA expression of citrate synthase. Nedosiran at a dose level of 20 mg/kg reduced citrate synthase mRNA expression (up to 50%) in rabbit maternal liver, but not in fetal liver. Since citrate synthase catalyzes the last step in the Krebs cycle, the inhibition could reduce ATP generation and lead to alterations in high energy dependent organs, such as the heart and skeletal muscle. However, lack of seed sequence complementarity of nedosiran in the maternal and fetal genes indicate that non-RNAi mechanisms may account for the higher post-implantation loss and lower number of live fetuses that occurred at \ge 6 mg/kg, and fetal cardiovascular and skeletal malformations at 20 mg/kg/dose. Human citrate synthase mRNA does not have sequence homology with nedosiran, but in humans, nedosiran inhibits LDHA, a key enzyme in the conversion of lactate to pyruvate, involved in glycolysis in the generation of ATP, and thus, theoretically, knockdown of LDHA and reduction of ATP generation in off-target organs could lead to crucial developmental abnormalities.
L683: Source Applicant's Nonclinical Study Report.
L684: Abbreviations: ATP, adenosine triphosphate; GD, gestational day; LDHA, lactate dehydrogenase A; RNA, RNA interference

- Maternal livers from DCR-m355a administered mice had a reduction of 5.8% in LDHA mRNA expression relative to controls, but there was no reduction in pup liver LDHA mRNA expression, suggesting no fetal or lactation exposure to DCR-m355a.
