L703: ## 13.3. Other Toxicology/Specialized Studies
L704: ## 13.3.1. Platelet Activation
L705: Due to concerns regarding platelet activation induced by the phosphorothioate oligonucleotides, the potential of nedosiran to activate human platelets was assessed ex vivo in whole blood using flow cytometry. Nedosiran at concentrations up to 200 µg/mL did not induce human platelet activation as shown by the absence of expression of CD62P. In addition, nedosiran did not affect platelet counts, prolong the activated thromboplastin time or activate complement in the in vivo toxicology studies in mice and monkeys.
L706: ## 13.3.2. Antidrug Antibodies (ADA)
L707: ADA analysis has not been conducted with samples from the nonclinical studies; however, PK and pharmacodynamics (PD) data from the nonclinical studies suggest that neutralizing ADAs against nedosiran were not formed. Repeat-dosing of nedosiran in monkeys showed robust reduction of the target LDHA mRNA. In addition, repeat dosing in mice and monkeys achieved similar maximum plasma concentration (C_{max}) and AUC after first and last dosing of nedosiran. Moreover, immune complex deposition in tissues was not observed in the repeat-dose toxicity studies of nedosiran in mice and monkeys.
L708: ## 13.3.3. Impurities/Degradants
L709: - The nedosiran drug substance tested in the toxicology studies was from batches used in the clinical drug product or manufactured using a similar process. The impurity levels in the pivotal repeat-dose toxicity studies were greater than those potentially present in the commercial product for clinical use based on the proposed drug substance specifications; therefore, the impurities and their proposed specifications are considered qualified.
- The [REDACTED](b) (4) impurities, and [REDACTED](b) (4) impurity that may be present as a degradation product in the drug product are qualified in the toxicology studies up to the proposed specifications. No more than [REDACTED](b) (4)% are proposed as [REDACTED](b) (4) acceptance criteria for qualification threshold. [REDACTED](b) (4)
L710: (b) (4) Overall, the drug substance-related impurities are considered qualified.
L711: ## 13.4. Individual Reviews of Studies Submitted With the New Drug Application
L712: Not applicable.
L713: ## 14. Clinical Pharmacology
L714: ## 14.1. In Vitro Studies
L715: ## 14.1.1. Plasma Protein Binding
L716: The plasma protein binding for nedosiran was determined using an electrophoretic mobility shift assay, which is based on the principle that an oligonucleotide-protein complex and an unbound oligonucleotide will have different mobility during electrophoresis. The conventional methods to determine binding were reported to have issues related to nonspecific adsorption. Though plasma protein binding was carried out with nedosiran concentrations ranging from 1 to 10 µg/mL, the electrophoretic mobility shift assay was not sensitive enough to quantify concentrations below 5 µg/mL. The incubations were done for 60 min at 37°C and 90% human plasma. Plasma protein binding was dependent on concentrations with the mean percentage binding decreasing from 85.6% to 73.9% for nedosiran concentrations of 5 and 10 µg/mL, respectively. These concentrations are approximately 5- to 10-fold higher than the clinical C_{max} of nedosiran.
L717: ## 14.1.2. Metabolism of Nedosiran
L718: The in vitro metabolism studies for nedosiran were carried out using human hepatocyte suspension. Nedosiran was stable without any metabolite formation after a 24 min incubation with human hepatocytes at concentrations ranging between 0.25 and 75 µg/mL, indicating no role for cytochrome P450 (CYP) or uridine 5'-diphospho-glucuronosyltransferase metabolism.
L719: ## 14.1.3. Inhibition and Induction of CYP Isozymes by Nedosiran
L720: The in vitro assessment of CYP enzyme inhibition was carried out in human hepatocytes with or without a 30 min preincubation of nedosiran at concentrations ranging between 0.25 µg/mL to 75 µg/mL. A cocktail of CYP substrates was added and CYP activity analysis was performed after 24 or 54 min (for 30 min preincubation).
L721: The induction potential of nedosiran on CYP enzymes were assessed at concentrations ranging from 0.25 to 75 µg/mL after a 48 h and 72 h incubation for mRNA and CYP activity evaluations, respectively.
L722: The results for inhibition and induction of CYP enzyme activity by nedosiran and positive controls are summarized in Table 58. Nedosiran is unlikely to be an inhibitor or inducer of CYP enzymes at clinically relevant plasma concentrations.

L743: ## 14.2. In Vivo Studies
L744: ## **DCR-PHXC-101 (Part A): Single Ascending Dose PK and PD Study in Healthy Volunteers and DCR-PHXC-101 (Part B): Single Ascending Dose PK and PD Study in PH1 Patients**
L745: ## Study Design
L746: This was a phase 1, placebo-controlled, single-blind, single-center study in normal healthy volunteers (Group A, N=25) and open-label multicenter study in patients with PH (Group B, N=18) to evaluate the safety, tolerability, pharmacokinetics and pharmacodynamics of single ascending subcutaneous doses of nedosiran. There were five cohorts in Group A, each of which comprised 5 healthy participants (3 active, 2 placebo) randomized to receive one of the following nedosiran sodium doses: 0.3, 1.5, 3, 6 or 12.0 mg/kg (0.28, 1.4, 2.8, 5.6, or 11.3 mg/kg free acid equivalent [FAE], respectively). There were three cohorts in Group B, in which 18 PH patients
L747: were assigned into cohorts of 6, 8 and 4 to receive single nedosiran sodium doses of 1.5, 3 and 6 mg/kg, respectively (1.4, 2.8 and 5.6 mg/kg FAE, respectively).
L748: Plasma PK was analyzed at predose, 0.083, 0.25, 0.5, 1, 2, 4, 6, 8, 12, 24, 48, 168, 336, 504, and 696 h (Day 29) postdose. Pooled urine was collected at 0 to 6, &gt;6 to 12, and &gt;12 to 24 h postdose and on Days 8, 15, 22, 43, 57, and every 28 days until urine oxalate returned to 80% of baseline values.
L749: ## Results
L750: In Group A (healthy participants), the peak, as well as the total exposure of nedosiran, was greater than dose-proportional with increase in dose in the 0.3 to 12 mg/kg dose range. In Group B (participants with PH), the exposure of nedosiran was dose proportional with mean C_{max} increasing 3.8-fold and mean AUC_{0-last} increasing 4.5-fold with a 4-fold change in dose from 1.5 mg/kg to 6 mg/kg. The amount of dose excreted unchanged in urine ranged between ~15% to 30% and ~22% to 35%, in Groups A and B, respectively. In Group B, the mean maximum percentage reduction in 24 h excretion of urinary oxalate from baseline across cohorts was 51%, 74%, and 78% for the 1.5, 3, and 6 mg/kg doses, respectively.

L764: ## **DCR-PHXC-102 (Intrinsic factor PK): Single Dose Study in Participants With Severe Renal Impairment and End-Stage Renal Disease (ESRD)**
L765: ## **Study Design**
L766: Study DCR-PHXC-102 was an open-label study conducted to characterize the effects of severe renal impairment and hemodialysis (in patients with ESRD) on the PK of nedosiran. The control group was healthy participants with normal renal function and was matched for gender, age (\pm 10 years), and weight (\pm 20\%) with the other test groups (Table 62). The participants received a single dose of nedosiran sodium (170 mg) by SC injection on Day 1 and serial PK samples were collected at predose, 0.25, 0.5, 1, 2, 4, 6, 8, 10, 12, 24, 48, 72, 96, 168, 336, and 672 h (Day 28) postdose. In Group 1a, blood samples collected during hemodialysis for PK assessment were drawn from both the line entering and the line exiting the dialyzer (representing arterial and venous blood samples, respectively). Dialysate samples in Group 1a were obtained every 30 min for the duration of dialysis on Day 1. Pooled urine (for participants with urine output) was obtained at predose (single void), 0 to 6, &gt;6 to 12, &gt;12 to 24, &gt;24 to 48, &gt;48 to 72, and &gt;72 to 96 h postdose.

L774: ## **Results**
L775: In participants with normal renal function, mean renal clearance was 3.2 L/h, and the mean unchanged drug eliminated over the first 96 h was 15.2%. Exposures increased with impairment of renal function, but the time to the maximum plasma concentration was not affected. During the 4 h dialysis period only 4% of drug was eliminated; however, overall exposure (AUC and Cmax) was 30% lower in Group 1a (participants who were dosed 8 h before hemodialysis), as compared to Group 1b (those dosed 2 h after hemodialysis) and the reasons for these observations are not clear.

L791: ## Conclusion
L792: The mean nedosiran plasma exposures increased approximately two-to-three-fold in severe renal impairment groups as compared to participants with normal renal function. According to the clinical team, the available data are not sufficient to provide dosing recommendations for patients with an estimated glomerular filtration rate (eGFR) &lt;30 mL/min/1.73 m2 or patients with kidney failure receiving dialysis because data on the effect of nedosiran on reducing plasma oxalate (i.e., an appropriate surrogate endpoint for the systemic manifestations of PH1 in patients with such low levels of kidney function) would be needed to do so.
L793: ## DCR-PHXC-103 (Intrinsic Factor PK): Single Dose Study in Healthy Japanese and Caucasian Participants
L794: ## Study Design
L795: Study DCR-PHXC-103 was an open-label, parallel-group, uncontrolled, single SC dose study to compare the PK of nedosiran between 18 Japanese and 18 Caucasian healthy participants (done as a part of market application in Japan). The participants were assigned to receive one of three dose levels of nedosiran sodium (FAE) as follows: 1.5 (1.4 FAE) mg/kg, 3.0 (2.8 FAE) mg/kg, or 6.0 (5.6 FAE) mg/kg with six participants per group per dose.
L796: Blood for determination of nedosiran concentrations and PK parameters was collected before dosing and at 5 min and at 0.25, 0.5, 1, 2, 4, 6, 8, 12, 24, 48, 72, 168 (Day 8), and 336 (Day 15) h postdose. Urine for determination of nedosiran concentrations and PK parameters was collected predose (single void) and over the intervals of 0 to 6 h, &gt;6 to 12 h, &gt;12 to 24 h, &gt;24 to 48 h (24 h urine collection), and &gt;48 to 72 h (24 h urine collection).
L797: ## Results
L798: The PK of nedosiran was similar between healthy Japanese and Caucasian participants for the tested dose range of 1.5 to 6 mg/kg (Table 65). The 90% confidence interval for the slope of the relationship between the log-transformed PK parameters and log-transformed dose contained 1 for both races and there was no evidence to exclude dose proportionality. Approximately 20% of the dose was excreted unchanged in the urine and the renal clearance or fraction excreted unchanged in urine were independent of dose and ethnicity. The renal clearance ranged between 2.3 to 3.9 L/h across the cohorts.

L811: ## **DCR-PHXC-201 (Double-Blind Efficacy Study): Multiple Dose Study with PK and PD in PH Patients (Children and Adults)**
L812: ## **Study Design**
L813: The study design is described in Section 6.2.1.1. Adult and adolescent participants weighing at least 50 kg received 6 nedosiran doses of 170 mg (160 mg FAE) administered monthly (or matching placebo), while those weighing less than 50 kg received nedosiran doses of 136 mg (128 mg FAE) administered monthly (or matching placebo). Participants who were 6 to 11 years old received a monthly nedosiran dose of 3.5 mg/kg (3.3 mg/kg FAE), but not to exceed 136 mg (128 mg FAE) in total dose (or matching placebo). The blood samples for PK were obtained as per the following schedules:
L814: - Age \ge18 years: Days 1 and 30: predose and at 5, 15, and 30 min and 1, 2, 4, 6, 10, and 12 h postdose; Days 2 and 31: 24 h postdose; Day 150: Predose and at 2, 6, and 12 h postdose.
- Age 6 to 17 years: Days 1 and 30: predose and at 30 min and 2 and 10 h postdose; Days 2 and 31: 24 h postdose; Day 150: predose and at 2 and 10 h postdose.
L815: ## **Results**
L816: The median body weight in adults, adolescent, and pediatric participants were 66, 59.6, and 33 kg, respectively. The median eGFR was higher in adolescent and pediatric patients (94 and 142 mL/min/m2, respectively) compared with the median eGFR in adult patients (79 mL/min/m2). Overall, there were 4 patients with moderate renal impairment (RI) and 7 patients with mild RI. Of these 11 patients with renal impairment, only 2 patients were adolescent patients with mild RI. A total of 33 participants completed study treatment and continued into the long-term extension study, Study DCR-PHXC-301.

L830: **Figure 9. Comparison of Exposures by Renal Function Category (Study DCR-PHXC-201) After Repeated Dosing With Nedosiran**
L831: Source: Figure 10 in Section 16.1.13 (PK and ADA report) of Study DCR-PHXC-201 Clinical Study Report.
L832: Abbreviations: AUC0-last, area under the concentration-time curve from time 0 to last quantifiable time point; Cmax, maximum observed concentration
L833: PK sampling for nedosiran in DCR-PHXC-201 did not extend beyond 24 h (Days 1, 30) or 12 h (Day 150). Given the limited PK sampling, the plasma exposure comparisons are limited to the first 12 to 24 h. In adults and adolescents, the plasma exposure of nedosiran did not seem to change with time suggesting no accumulation following repeated monthly dosing. There were very limited PK data in the 6- to 11-year-old pediatric participants. Based on the initial 12 to 24 h (Cmax) sampling period, no differences in the exposures were observed between participants with normal renal function and those with mild to moderate renal impairment.
L834: ## **Nedosiran PK and PD in Patients With Mild Hepatic Impairment (HI) (Pooled Data From Studies DCR-PHXC-101, -201, and -301)**
L835: The single dose (-101) and multiple dose (-201 and -301) studies permitted enrollment of PH patients with normal hepatic function or mild HI, as defined by the National Cancer Institute Organ Dysfunction Working Group criteria:
L836: - **Normal:** Total bilirubin (TB) and aspartate aminotransferase (AST) \le upper limit of normal (ULN)
- **Mild HI:** TB \le ULN and AST &gt; ULN; or ULN &lt; TB \le 1.5x ULN with any AST
- **Moderate HI:** 1.5x ULN &lt; TB \le 3x ULN with any AST level
- **Severe HI:** TB &gt; 3x ULN with any AST level.
L837: A total of five patients with mild HI were enrolled across these studies. No patients with moderate or severe HI were enrolled in these studies. Overall, the nedosiran plasma concentration-time profiles and the 24 h urinary oxalate-time profiles were similar in patients with normal hepatic function and mild HI, as shown in Figure 10 and Figure 11

Participants with unknown hepatic function (n=34) were excluded from the plot. Data consisted of participants from study DCR-PHXC-201 and DCR-PHXC-301 who received 170 mg dose. L843: ## 14.3. Bioanalytical Method Validation and Performance
L844: ## Quantification of Nedosiran in Plasma, Urine, and Dialysate
L845: A validated anion-exchange high-performance liquid chromatography method was used for determination of nedosiran in human plasma, urine, and dialysate. The same method was used for the quantification of nedosiran in all the clinical studies in this NDA submission, including DCR-PHXC-101, DCR-PHXC-102, DCR-PHXC-103, DCR-PHXC-201, and DCR-PHXC-301. The assay was based on hybridization of the antisense strand of nedosiran with a complementary peptide nucleic acid strand that was attached to a dye to enable fluorescence detection. The concentrations of nedosiran were evaluated by quantifying the antisense strand of nedosiran, and values were reported as the full-length double-stranded siRNA sodium salt. There was no analyte extraction or internal standard employed.

L862: ## Quantification of Oxalate in Urine
L863: Validation was done for the use of a commercial assay kit (Trinity Biotech Oxalate kits) for the colorimetric determination of oxalate in human urine from 50 to 2000\muM. The determination of urinary oxalate is based on its enzymatic oxidation with oxalate oxidase, followed by measurement at 580 nm. The pH of human urine samples was adjusted to between 2.5 and 5.

L868: ## Conclusion
L869: The methods for quantifying nedosiran in human plasma, urine, dialysate and for oxalate in human urine satisfied the method validation criterion in accordance with the FDA Guidance for Industry *M10 Bioanalytical Method Validation and Study Sample Analysis* (November 2022) and showed an acceptable performance during the sample analysis, as applicable, in clinical Studies-101, -102, -103, -201, and -301.
L870: ## 14.4. Immunogenicity Assessment—Impact of PK/PD, Efficacy, and Safety
L871: ADA to nedosiran were detected in serum samples using a validated enzyme-linked immunosorbent assay method. The method was used for detection and confirmation of ADAs using a 30 µL aliquot of human serum sample. Samples were analyzed in a tiered approach with a screening assay that identified 'potential positive' samples, followed by a confirmatory assay and titer assay on confirmed positive samples. The drug tolerances for 400 ng/mL and 1600 ng/mL of ADA were reported to be 1.00 µg/mL and 25.0 µg/mL of nedosiran (drug), respectively. No neutralizing assay was developed.
L872: ADA sampling was obtained from PH patients, across the trials, as below.
L873: - Study-101 (Part B): Days 1, 15, 57
- Study-201: Screening, Days 45, 180
- Study-301: Screening, Days 45, 180
L874: A total of 59 patients were screened with the ADA assay and none were identified to have developed treatment-emergent ADA. Two patients tested positive for ADA only at baseline. Another patient tested positive both prior to dosing and post nedosiran dosing and therefore was not considered to have treatment-emergent ADA. The titer of this patient's ADA test response continually reduced by 94% over the 12-month treatment period from 2560 at screening, to 1280 on Day 45, and 640 on Days 180 and 160 during the follow-up sample (in Month 12).
L875: The PK profiles (on Days 1, 30, and 150) and PD response of nedosiran (24 h urinary oxalate excretion) following 170 mg one monthly dosing regimen in Studies -201 and -301 were compared between patients that screened positive for ADA and others. As shown in Figure 12 and Figure 13, the PK profiles and 24 h urinary oxalate excretion profiles of the three patients who tested positive for ADA at screening or any time point during the study were comparable to other patients that tested negative for ADA.
L876: **Figure 12. Overlay Plot of Median Plasma Concentration-Time Profiles in PH1 Patients With and Without ADA in Studies-201/-301**
L877: Source: Figure 22, Population PKPD model report DIC202005 [253335].
Dashed line represents the LLOQ (1 ng/mL). Time was truncated to 24 h postdose; data below the LLOQ (1.00 ng/mL) were excluded; Y-axis on log scale.
Abbreviations: ADA, antidrug antibodies; LLOQ, lower limit of quantification; PH1, primary hyperoxaluria type 1; PKPD, pharmacokinetics/pharmacodynamics

Abbreviations: ADA, antidrug antibodies; BSA, body surface area; PH1, primary hyperoxaluria type 1; PKPD, pharmacokinetics/pharmacodynamics; \text{UO}_{24}, 24 h urinary oxalate
L880: ## Conclusion
L881: The presence of nedosiran antibodies did not affect the PK of nedosiran or PD response to nedosiran. However, only one patient had ADA while on treatment, thus data are limited. All three patients who were positive for ADA were positive at baseline; no patients developed treatment-emergent ADA.
L882: ## 14.5. Pharmacometrics Assessment
L883: ## Population PK Analysis
L884: The Applicant submitted two population PK and PK-PD study reports, ‘Population PKPD Model Update and Simulations for Nedosiran (DCR-PHXC)’ (referred to as ‘main report’) and ‘Supplement 1 to Population PKPD Model Update and Simulations for Nedosiran (DCR-PHXC)’ (referred to as ‘Supplement 1’). An additional 138 urine oxalate observations and 219 dosing records beyond 24 months from the start of the study were included in Supplement 1. There were no changes in PK data from studies DCR-PHXC-101, DCR-PHXC-102, DCRPHXC-103, DCR-PHXC-201 and DCR-PHXC-301. The model described in Supplement 1 was regarded as the final model. The final model building population PK dataset consisted of 379, 469, 429, 363, and 338 plasma nedosiran concentrations from studies DCR-PHXC-101, DCR-PHXC-102, DCR-PHXC-103, DCR-PHXC-201, and DCR-PHXC-301, respectively, with a total of 143 participants 9 to 73 years old, with body weight ranging from 31.8 to 132 kg. The dataset included a total of 91 males and 52 females. The demographics summary for all studies is provided in Table 72 and Table 73.

L893: Source: dic202005-253335-supp1, Table 5.
Abbreviation: PK, pharmacokinetics
L894: The nedosiran PK can be described by a two-compartment disposition model with a dual-transit absorption model to better describe the biphasic absorption of nedosiran when administered subcutaneously (Figure 14). A dual linear and nonlinear Michaelis-Menten model was implemented to describe the over proportional increase in PK exposure as dose increased. Between-subject variability was included on apparent central volume of distribution, absorption transit rate constant for the slow pathway, absorption transit rate constant for the fast pathway, maximum metabolic rate, and fraction of the dose absorbed via the slow pathway. The parameter estimates are shown in Table 74.

L902: Abbreviations: CV, coefficient of variation; eGFR, estimated glomerular filtration rate; PH1, primary hyperoxaluria type 1; PK, pharmacokinetics; RSE, relative standard error; WT, weight
L903: Allometric scaling by body weight was included on clearance (including apparent clearance from the central compartment, apparent intercompartmental clearance) and volume terms (including apparent central volume of distribution, apparent volume of distribution for the peripheral compartment) with exponents fixed to 0.75 and 1, respectively. Additionally, eGFR was included as a covariate on apparent clearance from the central compartment and apparent central volume of distribution. Body weight was found to be a significant covariate on absorption transit rate constant for the slow pathway and absorption transit rate constant for the fast pathway. In addition, a diagnosis of PH1 was found to be a significant covariate on absorption transit rate constant for the slow pathway. Vitamin B6 use at study start was tested and was not recognized as a significant covariate.
L904: The evaluation of the population PK model was based on the model described in the main report rather than the model described previously in supplement 1. The PK samples and model structure were identical for the two models with minor change in parameter estimates (fold-change &lt;5%). Plots of observed versus population predicted plasma concentrations and observed versus individual predicted plasma concentrations are presented below in Figure 15. Plots of the observed versus the population predicted concentrations demonstrated acceptable bias, with the majority of data evenly scattered around the line of unity. Plots of the observed concentrations versus the individual predicted concentrations, which account for random effects, were more tightly scattered around the line of unity. There appears to be no obvious bias as demonstrated by the trend line for the data directly overlaying the line of unity. Additional diagnostic plots of conditional weighted residuals are shown in Figure 16, which highlight that the final model fitted the data with relatively good precision and acceptable bias.

L910: The magnitude of the effect of the included covariates on nedosiran exposure was evaluated by deriving the fold change in AUC_{\tau,ss} and C_{max,ss} for each covariate relative to a reference subject. The reference subject was defined as a PH1 patient receiving 170 mg nedosiran once monthly (Q1M), with a body weight of 70 kg and normal renal function (eGFR 90 mL/min/1.73 m^2). A total of 1000 simulations were conducted for each covariate effect subgroup, with the magnitude of the covariate effect randomly sampled from a normal distribution using the uncertainty in the parameter estimates of each individual covariate effect from the final model, and were used to derive a 95% prediction interval (2.5th to 97.5th percentiles of the simulations) for each covariate group. The impact of covariates on the exposure metrics (AUC_{\tau,ss} and C_{max,ss}) are
L911: summarized in Figure 17 and Figure 18. The magnitude of the effect of covariates on PK exposures was minor (less than an approximately 0.25-fold change) for most covariates apart from participants with low and high weight, severe renal impairment, or ESRD, with the 95% prediction interval contained within the 50 to 200% range of the reference subject.

Dashed red or orange lines represent the 80-125% or 50-200% range of the reference group, respectively. The blue dots and error bars represent the median and 95% (2.5th to 97.5th percentiles of the simulations) prediction intervals (PI) based on 1000 simulated subjects within each group including uncertainty around the estimated covariate effect(s).

L920: ## **Population PKPD Model**
L921: The PKPD model was developed based on 588 24 h urinary oxalate corrected for body surface area observations from 46 PH1 patients (of which 16 were rollover participants from Study DCR-PHXC-301) in Studies DCR-PHXC-101, DCR-PHXC-201, and DCR-PHXC-301. The 46 patients were 9 to 46 years old, with body weight ranging from 35 to 116 kg. A total of 21 males and 25 females were included in the studies. The demographics summary for all studies is provided in Table 75.

L929: An indirect response model with an effect compartment was used to describe the 24 h urinary oxalate (UO24) data. The model used the predicted individual concentrations from the population PK model at the effect compartment to drive the effect on UO24, with the drug effect
L930: of nedosiran modeled as an Imax model inhibiting the production of UO24. A Hill coefficient (\gamma) was estimated to describe the shape of the concentration-effect relationship. Between-subject variability terms were included on baseline UO24, half-maximal inhibitory concentration, and maximum inhibitory concentration (Figure 19). An additive error model was used to describe the residual unexplained variability of UO24. The covariate analysis revealed no statistical evidence for any covariate effects. Parameter estimates from the final population PKPD model of nedosiran are reported in Table 76.

L949: ## Reviewer's Comment
L950: *The reviewer was able to reproduce the Applicant's proposed population PK model and PKPD model. The proposed models appear to be acceptable. Renal function (normal, mild RI, and moderate RI) was identified as a covariate for the population PK model, whereas eGFR was not identified as significant for the PKPD model. Hepatic function (normal and mild HI), age, sex, and race/ethnicity were not statistically significant covariates in either the population PK model or the PKPD model. Of note, no data from pediatric patients &lt;9 years of age were included in PKPD model development. Hence, the effect of age on the PKPD relationship in pediatric patients below 9 years of age remains uncertain.*
L951: ## Population PK Simulation
L952: To predict nedosiran and 24 h urinary oxalate concentrations corrected for body surface area following administration of nedosiran in pediatric (aged 6 to &lt;12 years), adolescent (aged ≥12 to &lt;18 years) and adult (aged ≥18 to &lt;65 years) patients with PH1, virtual subject databases were created. The pediatric virtual subject database (n=1000) was generated for (virtual) children aged 6 to &lt;12 years. For each participant ≥10 years, body weight was simulated from the appropriate World Health Organization weight-for-age tables according to age and sex using a Z-score sampled from the normal distribution with a mean of zero and SD of 1. For each participant &gt;10 years, body weight was randomly sampled from the Continuous National Health and
L953: Nutrition Examination Survey database for a matching age and sex. Clinical data in adult participants suggests that a significant proportion of nedosiran is eliminated via renal excretion. Therefore, a maturation function that describes the increase in glomerular filtration rate with age was included in the derivation of eGFR used in the pediatric simulations. First, an eGFR was randomly generated for each pediatric participant from a uniform distribution between the ranges specific for the relevant renal function category. Second, a maturation function (Figure 23) was applied to the randomly generated eGFR which is individualized for the virtual participants' age.
L954: ## **Figure 23. Maturation Function**
L955: F_{GFR} = \frac{PMA^{3.4}}{47.6^{3.4} + PMA^{3.4}}
L956: Source:
L957: Postmenstrual age in weeks (PMA) is calculated as postnatal age plus 40 weeks as age in the virtual subject database commences at birth (age =0).
L958: The simulations were replicated for participants with the RI categories outlined below:
L959: - Normal renal function: 90 – &lt;150 mL/min/1.73 m2
- Mild renal impairment: 60 – &lt;90 mL/min/1.73 m2
- Moderate renal impairment: 30 – &lt;60 mL/min/1.73 m2
L960: Simulated nedosiran concentration-time profiles for patients with age \ge 12 years and body weight \ge 50 kg following the first dose stratified by renal function are displayed in Figure 24. The PK exposure metrics (AUC_{\tau,ss} and C_{max,ss}) of the simulated dosing regimens in adolescents and adults with PH1 with weight \ge 50 kg, stratified by renal function are shown in Figure 25 and Figure 26. The AUC and C_{max} increased with decreasing renal function.

L1008: ## Reviewer's Comment
L1009: The minimum age of participants involved in the population PK model development was 9 years. Given that maturation of renal and hepatic function is close to complete by 6 years of age and a maturation function for eGFR was included in the population PK model simulation, the estimated nedosiran PK profile down to 6 years of age is acceptable.
L1010: Based on population PK simulations, the C_{max} at steady state is higher for patients 6 to &lt;12 years of age and patients of age \ge 12 years and body weight &lt;50 kg as compared to the reference group of patients of age \ge 12 years and body weight \ge 50 kg for each of the corresponding categories of renal function (normal, mild RI, and moderate RI). In contrast, the AUC at steady state for the three age groups is similar. The model further indicates that PK exposures (C_{max} and AUC) increase as renal function (normal, mild RI, and moderate RI) decreases.
L1011: The Reviewer summarized the fold-change in C_{max} and AUC at steady state for different age and renal function groups using only patients aged \ge 12 years with a body weight \ge 50 kg and normal renal function as reference (Table 77). The steady state C_{max} for the pediatric patients aged 6 to &lt;12 years with normal, mild RI, and moderate RI increased by 76%, 99%, and 125%, respectively, compared to the reference. For patients with age \ge 12 years with body weight &lt;50 kg, the fold-change in C_{max} varied from approximately 1.5 to 2. The steady state AUC had relatively less of an increase compared with C_{max}.
L1012: The clinical team has confirmed that the safety profile was acceptable in the three healthy volunteers who received a single dose of 12 mg/kg nedosiran in DCR-PHXC-101, which resulted in a C_{max} of approximately 3000 ng/mL and, according to the clinical team, no major safety
L1013: concerns have been identified in the nedosiran clinical development program to date. Hence, the higher PK exposures in pediatric patients 6 to &lt;12 years and patients with body weight &lt;50 kg and RI do not raise a safety concern. Of note, the approved indication will only be for patients 9 years of age and older because of lack of efficacy and safety data in patients younger than 9 years.

L1017: ## Population PKPD Simulation
L1018: PKPD simulations were performed under scenarios identical to those discussed in the population PK simulation section. The limit of normal and near-normal limit were set to &lt;460\mumol/24 h and &lt;600\mumol/24 h, respectively. The time courses of simulated 24 h urine oxalate under the proposed dosing scenarios stratified by age are displayed in Figure 34 for normal renal function, Figure 35 for mild RI, and Figure 36 for moderate RI. The percentages of simulated PH1 patients within the normal and near normal ranges at the end of Weeks 13, 26, 39, 52, and 128 are displayed in Table 78, Table 79, and Table 80. Overall, there were no major differences across the age groups and RI categories.

L1045: ## Reviewer's Comment
L1046: As discussed elsewhere in this review, following subcutaneous administration, nedosiran is delivered to the hepatocyte, its site of action. Because the plasma concentration of nedosiran does not reflect its concentration in the liver, the efficacy of nedosiran is not directly correlated with plasma concentration and PKPD simulations were used to further evaluate the impact of age, body weight and renal function on efficacy. Based on the Applicant's simulations, the PD response appeared to be similar among the subgroups stratified by age, body weight, and renal function.
L1047: The reviewer also performed independent PKPD simulations with representative patients from each subgroup. The simulated body weights for patients \ge 12 years of age were 30, 40, 49, 50, 70, and 100 kg. For pediatric patients &lt;12 years of age, the average body weights for boys and girls were based on the Centers for Disease Control growth chart for the corresponding age: 21 kg for a 6-year-old, 26 kg for an 8-year-old, and 31 kg for a 10-year-old. The eGFR values that were used were 30, 45, 60, 75, and 90 mL/min/1.72 m^2. The representative patients were generated by combining the different values for age, body weight, and eGFR. The reference patient was a patient with age of 30-year-old, body weight of 70 kg, and eGFR of 90 mL/min/1.72 m^2. The simulation results were consistent with the Applicant's, i.e., showed a similar PD response among the subgroups. (Figure 37, Figure 38, Figure 39, Figure 40, and Figure 41).
L1048: Because only one pediatric patient &lt;12 years of age (a 9-year-old patient) was included in the PKPD model development, the effect of age on the PKPD relationship in pediatric patients 6 to 9 years of age remains uncertain.

L1077: ## 16. Efficacy
L1078: ## 16.1. Random Highs and Lows From Use of AUC to Calculate Mean Percentage Change in Uox
L1079: Use of AUC to calculate mean change at steady-state will generate noise that is noninformative to clinical outcome. In the analyses shown in Table 81, the last three measurements from participant (b) (6) were used to provide a case study of the statistical properties of AUC to calculate percentage change from baseline urinary oxalate (Uox). The rows in Table 81 differ only in the order in which measurements were taken. Although the mean percentage change in Uox is exactly the same for each row, equal to -4%, the magnitudes and directions of the AUC vary. For a sufficiently large trial, the AUCs will average out to provide a useful point estimate of percentage change – this can be seen by taking the average of the six AUCs in Table 81 and dividing by 60, the number of days over which AUC was calculated; the result is the -4% seen as the average AUC in each row. However, for small sample sizes, such “averaging out” will be less likely to occur, and the use of AUC as a measure of percent change may increase uncertainty in the calculated mean Uox and, as shown in the last row of Table 81, AUC may not even reflect the sign of percentage change.
L1080: **Table 81. Dependence of AUC on Time Sequence of Collected Data**
L1081: AUC Replicate	Timepoint	AUC
Percentage Change Uox
Day 120	Day 150	Day 180	
Replicate 1	45%	-30%	-27%	-630.00
Replicate 2	45%	-27%	-30%	-585.00
Replicate 3	-27%	45%	-30%	495.00
Replicate 4	-27%	-30%	45%	-630.00
Replicate 5	-30%	-27%	45%	-585.00
Replicate 6	-30%	45%	-27%	495.00
L1082: Source: Reviewer program endpoints and auc.xls, data from participant (b) (6).
L1083: Abbreviations: AUC, area under the concentration-time curve; Uox, urinary oxalate
L1084: The Applicant's use of AUC implicitly uses single-imputation of missing data to measure percent change in urinary oxalate. The Applicant's use of such imputation contradicts recommendations by the National Academy of Sciences. Figure 43 illustrates this point (i.e., that such single imputation is implicit in the Applicant's calculation of AUC). In Figure 43, AUC from Days 90 to 180 is calculated as an area from four actual data points (in blue) observed on Days 90, 120, 150, and 180, and with values for the 87 other days after Day 90 (within red box) imputed to lie on line-segments connecting the two nearest measured endpoints.
L1085: **Figure 43. Applicant's Calculation of AUC Using Single Imputation. Measured Data Points in Blue**
L1086: Source: Adapted from Figure 5 of the protocol.
Abbreviation: AUC, area under the concentration-time-curve
L1087: The imputed data point for data missing on each day is then calculated as a single imputation using linear interpolation between the values of the adjacent two data points actually observed. However, as discussed below, such single imputation fails to take into account variability inherent in the imputation and, if the mean of the imputed values is not equal to the mean of the interpolated data point, the calculated value of AUC may not reflect the actual mean percentage change in UO24 experienced by each patient over the specified time period.
L1088: To examine the validity of the Applicant's single imputation method, one can make use of actual data from UO24 collected by the Applicant. Data were collected at first screening and 30 days after randomization. Data were also collected at intermediate time points, between first screening and 30 days and, for such intermediate timepoints, one can examine the correspondence between singly-imputed and actual values – if single linear imputation between data collected between the first and the 30-day measurement is correct, the measured and imputed data will be the same.
L1089: However, measured and imputed data are not the same. In particular, all of the single imputations used in the Applicant's calculation of AUC inappropriately reduced the magnitude of the percent change in urinary oxalate concentration for each participant (Table 82). A linear regression on the data in Table 82 indicates a nominally significant relationship between observed data as a function of singly imputed data for the calculation of AUC (p=0.04); however the slope is only 0.06, with only 20% of the variance in the observed data explained by the Applicant's imputation.
L1090: **Table 82. Single Imputation for AUC vs. Observed Values of 24 h Urinary Oxalate for Data Collected Between First Screening and Day 30**
L1091: Participant
(b) (6)	N	24-hour Urinary Oxalate
Single Imputation for AUC	Observed
1	0.3	4.8
1	2.1	-18.9
1	-0.3	14.8
1	0.0	-21.8
1	-0.2	-0.6
L1092: Source: Reviewer program auc imputation urinary oxalate.sas.
L1093: Abbreviations: AUC, area under the concentration-time curve; N, number of participants
L1094: The single linear-interpolation-based imputations used by the Applicant altered the mean and standard deviation of percentage change. As an example, Table 83 shows observed data from subject (b) (6) collected on Days 120, 150, and 180 and, like AUC, applies interpolation to the intermediate points. The calculated mean and standard deviation of percentage change from Baseline is altered according to the number of imputations conducted. According to the original data (top row of Table 83), the mean percentage change in UO24 is -4.00 and the standard deviation is 42.46. Adding a single linear interpolation imputation halfway between each observed data point (second row), the mean change in UO24 is reduced from -4.00 to -6.60 and the standard deviation is reduced from 42.46 to 32.81. Adding yet another imputation (third row) further decreases the calculated mean and standard deviation. AUC calculations by the Applicant over 90 days may be considered as involving even more such imputations, adding 87 data points to the 4 originally observed.
L1095: **Table 83. Impact of Single Linear Imputation on Mean and Variance of Percentage Change in 24 h Uox Given the Same AUC***
L1096: Imputation	Observed and Imputed Values for Percentage Change in Uox	Mean	St Dev
Original data	45 -30	-27 -4.00	42.46
One midpoint	45 7.5 -30	-28.5 -27 -6.60	32.81
Two midpoints	45 26.25 7.5 -11.25 -30	-29.25 -28.5 -27.75 -27 -8.33	28.15
L1097: Source: Reviewer program endpoints and auc.xls.
L1098: * Calculated AUC=-630.00 for all three rows.
L1099: Abbreviations: AUC, area under the concentration-time curve; St Dev, standard deviation
L1100: To summarize, the statistical properties of AUC of percentage change and its relationship to mean percentage change have not yet been established. Unlike mean percentage change, the value of AUC may vary according to the temporal sequence in which the underlying values of percent change are recorded and, as a consequence, in small trials, such as the present one, AUC is more likely to generate random highs and lows in mean outcome that are not clinically relevant. Further, as shown in Table 83, in which the AUC values for all rows are the same, equal to 630, a single value of AUC may map to several values of mean percentage change, i.e., there is no one-to-one correspondence between AUC and mean percentage change. The Applicant's calculation of AUC also involves single imputation of missing values, in direct violation of recommendations by the National Academy of Sciences, and the imputation method used, linear interpolation, produces results that do not match collected data (Table 82). For these reasons, AUC is not an ideal endpoint to use in clinical trials when the clinical question of interest aligns with mean percentage change.
L1101: ## 16.2. Adaptive Randomization and Assessment of Statistical Significance
L1102: For the adaptive randomization, each enrolling patient was first identified according to one of the six strata defined by stratum elements age (two categories) and eGFR (three categories). Next, the hypothetical observed (if that participant were assigned to a particular treatment) and expected (according to the planned 2:1 randomization ratio) number of participants in each stratum element were compared, with the difference recorded for each treatment. The difference for each stratification factor was then weighted by 0.33 (placebo goal of 2:1 randomization) given hypothetical assignment to placebo or by 0.67 given hypothetical assignment to nedosiran, and then the weighted differences were summed for each treatment over both stratum elements to form an imbalance score, with the enrolled participant assigned with an 80% probability to the treatment which resulted in the lowest imbalance score. For the first participant, or for participants in which the imbalance scores were equal, treatments were randomly assigned according to the nominal 2:1 randomization ratio.
L1103: Given the adaptive randomization, rerandomization tests are preferable when testing for statistical significance. To conduct such tests, 50 new randomizations were first conducted with randomized arm assigned to each individual according to the Applicant's adaptive randomization scheme. Then, as in the current submission, multiple imputation of missing data was used to create 50 datasets for each rerandomized trial, with each dataset analyzed using the original mixed model repeated measures. The results for each rerandomized trial were then combined using Rubin's rule to obtain confidence limits and p-values.
L1104: Rerandomization tests performed in this manner confirmed the statistical significance of the primary endpoint results. To provide adequate precision in the estimated p-values, at least 10,000 rerandomizations were needed. The analysis documented here was based on 13,500 trial rerandomizations and yielded a p-value of 0.001 when participants with PH1 or PH2 were analyzed, and a p-value of &lt;0.001 when participants with PH1 were analyzed.
L1105: Treatment with nedosiran also reduced absolute Uox excretion (Table 84).

L1112: ## 16.3. Potential Impact of Randomization Imbalances According to Race
L1113: All Asian participants in this trial were assigned to the nedosiran arm. Although there is no reason to believe that participants who were Asian might have a larger response than other groups, analyses were conducted excluding Asian participants. When the data were reanalyzed with Asian participants excluded, the least square estimated magnitude of treatment effect and (nominal) statistical significance were retained (Table 85).

L1120: ## 16.4. Potential Impact of Randomization to Larger Than Labeled Adult Doses
L1121: As mentioned in the footnote to Table 84, dosing for adults weighing &lt;50 kg was changed during the trial; in Version 4 of the protocol released on April 20, 2019, dosing for this group of adults was changed from 170 mg monthly to 137 mg monthly. Two such adults received the higher dose before the dosing change, and no such adults were enrolled after the dosing change. Without the two adults weighing less than 50 kg who received a higher dose than that proposed in labeling, the magnitude of the treatment effect and (nominal) statistical significance of the primary endpoint were retained (Table 86).

L1128: ## 16.5. Potential Impact of Missing Data on Analysis Results
L1129: Sensitivity analysis to account for potential inaccuracies in the imputation of missing data should address a broad range of 'extreme' scenarios in which the investigational treatment has no or even negative effects on outcome among participants missing data. The Applicant did not provide extensive sensitivity analyses and so these analyses were conducted by the review team.
L1130: Tipping point sensitivity analyses were conducted by the review team using multiple imputation, with 50 datasets in which missing data (Table 11) were imputed for Uox normalized by body surface area for participants \le 18 years of age, as in the primary analysis, at Days 90, 120, 150, or 180. Imputations were conducted using multiple regression with the following independent variables: treatment, age category, eGFR category, and day. For each of these datasets, specified shifts were applied to the imputed values for each treatment arm, and these shifted values were used to calculate for each trial participant a new value for UO_{24} AUC \Delta\% Days 90 to 180. These AUC values were then analyzed for each imputed dataset, with results combined for each set of shifts using Rubin's method.
L1131: The tipping point analyses confirmed the robustness of the results to assumptions on missing data for the combined PH1 and PH2 participants (Table 87) and for the PH1 only participants (Table 88), with statistical significance retained for all plausible shifts in urinary oxalate excretion in missing data. The results provided in these tables show the impact on the treatment effect of shifts in the treatment arm ranging from 0 to an additional 1000\mumol/day, in which outcomes in missed assessments reflect increases rather than decreased urinary oxalate excretion in the nedosiran arm, and concurrently assess shifts in the placebo arm ranging from 0 to -1000 \mumol/day, in which outcomes in missed assessments reflect decreases rather than lack of change for urinary oxalate excretion in the placebo arm.

L1140: ## 16.6. Discrepant Results From Various Methodologies to Evaluate Uox Percentage Change From Baseline
L1141: The Applicant proposed to include information in Section 14 of the label on the percent change from baseline in urinary oxalate; however the Applicant used various methods of calculation which produced conflicting results. Results shown in the first row of Table 89 (b) (4), in which estimates of 24 h Uox AUC change from Baseline over Days 90 to 180 were divided by 90 days to obtain a percent change in AUC at steady-state. However, the 14.6% increase in the placebo arm is inconsistent with the trend graph (b) (4) (Figure 44), which indicates a decrease from Baseline among placebo patients from Days 90 to 180 rather than an increase. Further, both of these estimates conflict with the results shown in Table A4 of the Applicant's clinical study report, which are included in the second row of Table 89. In this analysis, the increase in urinary oxalate in the placebo arm is less than that obtained using the AUC-based method (b) (4), but is still inconsistent with what is seen in the trend graph.
L1142: **Table 89. Alternative Methods for Percentage Change in 24 h Urinary Oxalate Concentration, Days 90 to 180, PH1 mITT**
L1143: Source	Placebo	Nedosiran	Differencea
(95% CI)

L1146: Abbreviations: AUC, area under the concentration-time curve; CI, confidence interval; CSR, clinical study report; mITT, modified intent-to-treat; PH1, primary hyperoxaluria type 1
L1147: Figure 44. Mean (\pmStandard Error) Percentage Change From Baseline Urinary Oxalate, PH1 mITT
L1148: Source: (b) (4), adapted from analysis for CSR Figure 14.4.4a.
Abbreviations: CSR, clinical study report; mITT, modified intent-to-treat; PH1, primary hyperoxaluria type 1
L1149: Given the aforementioned discrepancies, it is worthwhile to consider the various approaches used by the Applicant. The estimate shown in the first row of Table 89 was obtained by dividing the change from baseline AUC by the number of days over which the AUC was calculated, a method that has not yet been studied in the statistical literature and that has undesirable statistical properties in small studies (see discussion of random highs and lows in Section 16.1 of this review). The mixed model repeated measures analysis shown in Table A4 in the clinical study report is shown in the last row of Table 89. This analysis included the following independent factors: treatment, baseline urinary oxalate concentration, age group, eGFR group, and study day (90, 120, 150, or 180), but the results conflict with the data used to generate Figure 44 which, in turn, failed to include imputation for missing data and calculated point estimates using simple averages, without any adjustments for covariates.
L1150: To address these issues with the approaches used by the Applicant, the review team implemented a single comprehensive intent-to-treat analysis which imputed 50 datasets for 24 h Uox measurements at Days 0, 30, 60, 90, 120, 150, and 180 and then evaluated the mean change from baseline in 24 h urinary oxalate over Days 90, 120, 150, and 180 for each imputed dataset using mixed model repeated measures with independent factors of treatment, eGFR group, age group, Baseline 24 h Uox concentration, and visit by treatment interaction, with a compound symmetric covariance matrix, with the results subsequently combined using Rubin's rule.

L1204: ## 22. Data Integrity-Related Consults (Office of Scientific Investigations, Other Inspections)
L1205: One clinical site inspection (Dr. John Lieske; Site 101) was completed by the Office of Scientific Investigations. The clinical site was chosen using a risk-based approach primarily based on the number of enrolled participants (7 of 35 enrolled participants), large treatment effect on the primary endpoint, and prior inspectional history. Based on the results of this inspection, the study (Protocol DCR-PHXC-201) appears to have been conducted adequately, and the clinical data generated by this site appears to be reliable in support of this NDA. See the Office of Scientific Investigations memorandum for further details.
L1206: ## 23. Labeling: Key Changes and Considerations
L1207: This Prescribing Information (PI) review includes a high-level summary of the rationale for major changes to the finalized PI as compared to the Applicant's draft PI (Table 96). The PI was reviewed to ensure that it meets regulatory/statutory requirements, is consistent (if appropriate) with labeling guidance, conveys clinically meaningful and scientifically accurate information needed for the safe and effective use of the drug, and provides clear and concise information for the healthcare practitioner.

- Patient Information
- Instructions for Use – Vial
- Instructions for Use – Prefilled Syringe
L1211: ## 24. Postmarketing Requirements and Commitments
L1212: These are the postmarketing requirements:
L1213: - Complete a 26-week GLP carcinogenicity study of nedosiran and murine surrogate (DCR-m355) by subcutaneous injection in TgRasH2 mice.
- Complete a long-term (2-year) GLP carcinogenicity study of nedosiran by subcutaneous injection in one rodent species.

L1262: Bar chart showing the distribution of Baseline Urinary Oxalate (Count) for Nedosiran (red bars) and Placebo (blue bars). The x-axis ranges from 0 to 4000, marked at 1000, 2000, 3000, and 4000. The y-axis is Count, ranging from 0 to 4. The chart shows that Nedosiran has a higher count in the lower ranges (1000-2000) compared to Placebo, while Placebo has a higher count in the higher ranges (3000-4000).
L1263: Figure 3 is a forest plot titled "Percentage Change From Baseline Urinary Oxalate by Subgroup. Frequentist and Bayesian Shrinkage Analyses, PH1 and PH2 Participants". The plot displays the Mean and 95% Confidence Interval (CI) for the percentage change from baseline urinary oxalate for various demographic subgroups, comparing Frequentist analysis (thinner lines) and Bayesian Shrinkage analysis (thicker lines).
The X-axis represents the Mean percentage change, ranging from -150 to 0. The Y-axis lists the subgroups analyzed:
Age: 6-11 year olds (Frequentist analysis and Shrinkage analysis)
Age: 12-17 year olds (Frequentist analysis and Shrinkage analysis)
Age: 18 years and above (Frequentist analysis and Shrinkage analysis)
Sex: Females (Frequentist analysis and Shrinkage analysis)
Sex: Males (Frequentist analysis and Shrinkage analysis)
The plot shows that the estimated treatment effect (Mean) is consistently negative across all subgroups, indicating a reduction in urinary oxalate. The Bayesian shrinkage analyses generally show a smaller magnitude of the mean effect compared to the frequentist analyses, particularly for the 6-11 year olds subgroup, where the frequentist mean is around -100, and the shrinkage mean is around -75. The 95% CIs for all subgroups include zero, suggesting no statistically significant difference in the treatment effect across the subgroups.
L1264: Figure 4 is a forest plot illustrating the Percentage Change From Baseline in Urinary Oxalate by Subgroup, comparing Frequentist and Bayesian Shrinkage Analyses for PH1 Participants. The plot displays the Mean and 95% Confidence Interval (CI) for different subgroups.
The subgroups analyzed are:
Age: 6-11 year olds, 12-17 year olds, 18 years and above.
Sex: Females, Males.
The plot shows that for all subgroups, the Bayesian Shrinkage Analysis (blue squares) generally yields a smaller magnitude of percentage change compared to the Frequentist Analysis (orange squares), and the Bayesian analysis also shows a tighter 95% CI.
Approximate values for Mean and 95% CI (based on visual estimation):
Subgroup	Analysis Type	Mean (%)	95% Lower CI (%)	95% Upper CI (%)

L1265: Scatter plot showing Maximum Post-baseline ALT or AST (xULN) on the X-axis versus Maximum Post-baseline Bilirubin (xULN) on the Y-axis for the Safety Population (DCR-PHXC-201). Data points are plotted for Nedosiran (blue dots) and Placebo (open circles). A dashed horizontal line at 2.0 xULN indicates the threshold for Potential Hy's Law cases. A dashed vertical line at 3.0 xULN indicates the threshold for Temple's Corollary. The plot shows that most patients have values below these thresholds, with a few outliers near the 2.0 xULN line.
L1266: Scatter plot showing Maximum Post-baseline Bilirubin (xULN) versus Maximum Post-baseline ALT or AST (xULN). The plot includes a dashed line at 2.0 xULN for Bilirubin and a vertical dashed line at 3.0 xULN for ALT/AST. The plot is divided into four quadrants: Cholestasis (top left), Potential Hy's Law (top right), and Temple's Corollary (bottom right). Data points are plotted for Placebo to Nedosiran PH (blue circles) and Nedosiran to Nedosiran PH (red squares). A single red square (Nedosiran to Nedosiran PH) is located in the Potential Hy's Law quadrant, indicating a potential case of drug-induced liver injury.
L1267: Figure 7 displays three panels showing median plasma concentration-time profiles for Rivfloza (nedosiran) across different doses and populations.
The Y-axis for all panels is Concentration (ng/mL), plotted on a logarithmic scale (1 to 3000). The X-axis is Time After Dose (hours).
The legend indicates four doses (mg/kg): 0.3 (n=3), 3 (n=3), 1.5 (n=3), and 6 (n=3).
Top Panel (Healthy Participants, 672 h): Shows rapid absorption and elimination. Concentrations peak around 1 hour and decline quickly, reaching near the LLOQ (1 ng/mL) by 24 hours.
Middle Panel (Patients With PH in Group A, First 48 h): Shows sustained concentrations, particularly for the 3 mg/kg and 6 mg/kg doses, which remain above 100 ng/mL for the first 48 hours. The 1.5 mg/kg dose peaks around 100 ng/mL and declines rapidly.
Bottom Panel (Patients With PH in Group B, First 48 h): Shows sustained concentrations, particularly for the 3 mg/kg and 6 mg/kg doses, which remain above 100 ng/mL for the first 48 hours. The 1.5 mg/kg dose peaks around 100 ng/mL and declines rapidly.
L1268: Figure 8 is a log-linear plot showing Mean (±SEM) Plasma Nedosiran Concentration (ng/mL) on the Y-axis versus Time (h) on the X-axis. The Y-axis is logarithmic, ranging from 1 to 1000. Three groups are plotted: Group 1a (Severe RI/ESRD on hemodialysis, nedosiran 8h before dialysis), Group 1b (Severe RI/ESRD on hemodialysis, nedosiran 2h after dialysis), and Group 3 (Participants with normal renal function). All groups show a rapid initial decline in concentration, followed by a slower, steady-state phase. Group 1a and Group 1b maintain significantly higher steady-state concentrations compared to Group 3.

Multiple lines represent different patient profiles defined by Age, Body Weight (BW), Estimated Glomerular Filtration Rate (eGFR), and Dose. The lines show a rapid decrease in urine oxalate rate over the first 12 weeks, stabilizing around 600 mcg/mol/24h. A black solid line represents the reference patient (Age \ge 12 years, BW=70kg, eGFR=90mL/min/1.73m^2). A dashed line at 600 mcg/mol/24h is labeled "near normal". A dashed line at 460 mcg/mol/24h is labeled "normal".

L1302: Figure 43 is a graph showing the percentage change in UO24 (Y-axis, ranging from -50% to 10%) versus Study Day (X-axis, ranging from 0 to 180). The graph illustrates the Applicant's calculation of AUC using single imputation. The area under the curve is shaded yellow. A red box highlights the region between Study Day 90 and 180, where the percentage change is negative (below 0%). Several blue dots represent measured data points, and the curve connecting them shows a sharp decline in the highlighted region.
L1303: Line graph showing Mean (±Standard Error) Percentage Change From Baseline Urinary Oxalate (Uox) over time (Visit) for Placebo (N=11) and TRADE NAME (N=17) groups. The Y-axis ranges from -70 to 40. The X-axis shows visits: Baseline, Day 30, Day 60, Day 90, Day 120, Day 150, and Day 180 (EOS). The Placebo group shows minimal change, hovering near 0% change. The TRADE NAME group shows a significant decrease in urinary oxalate, starting around -25% at Day 30, dropping to approximately -45% at Day 60, stabilizing around -50% from Day 90 to Day 120, and slightly decreasing to around -60% at Day 150 and Day 180.
